This is a glossary, not a\\r\\nspec. Implementation lives in the skills and scripts; decisions live in\\r\\n`decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Language\\r\\n\\r\\n### Paying and scheduling\\r\\n\\r\\n**Package**:\\r\\nFive or ten hours of tutoring bought upfront through Stripe, with\\r\\nbetween-session coaching included.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Bundle, plan, subscription, credits (credits are the pass\'s units, not the product)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Booking pass**:\\r\\nThe prepaid entitlement a student holds when sessions are **owed** to them,\\r\\ncarrying the count owed and the count used. Reachable at `/book/<slug>`.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Booking link (that is the pass\'s URL, not the thing), booking access, pass token\\r\\n\\r\\n**Owed sessions**:\\r\\nSessions a student has paid for and not yet taken. The booking pass is the\\r\\n**only** record of these; if no pass exists, nothing is owed.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Balance, remaining sessions, package balance (there is no separate balance model)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Arrangement**:\\r\\nWhat a student actually bought, and when. A ten-hour package on a given date,\\r\\nor pay-per-session at a given rate. Authored on the student\'s page, durable,\\r\\nand never rewritten: it is history, not state.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Plan, deal, terms\\r\\n\\r\\n**Live status**:\\r\\nThe regenerated mirror of a student\'s current backend state, including how many\\r\\nowed sessions remain. Lives in `live-status.generated.md`, is rebuilt from the\\r\\nbackend, and is never hand-edited or hand-corrected.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Balance sheet, status page\\r\\n\\r\\n**Single session**:\\r\\nOne tutoring session paid for individually through a Stripe-linked Cal.com\\r\\nlink, rather than out of a package. Needs no booking pass.\\r\\n_Avoid_: One-off, ad hoc session, hourly\\r\\n\\r\\n**Three-tier offer**:\\r\\nThe standard offer presented after a consultation: a single session at $45 per\\r\\nhour, a five-hour package at $40 per hour, and a ten-hour package at $35 per\\r\\nhour. Each offer carries its own verified action link. A prospect-specific\\r\\narrangement agreed during the consultation replaces the standard offer\\r\\nentirely.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Pricing menu, universal rate, package discount table\\r\\n\\r\\n**Consultation**:\\r\\nA free introductory call, booked through the Cal.com complimentary link. Does\\r\\nnot make the person a student.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Intro call, discovery call, free session\\r\\n\\r\\n**Consultation follow-up**:\\r\\nA message to a prospect who completed a consultation and has not paid. Payment\\r\\nends the consultation follow-up stage and moves the person into onboarding. The\\r\\nmessage presents the three tutoring offers so the prospect can choose how to\\r\\nbegin.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Onboarding email, post-payment email, student follow-up, application form email\\r\\n\\r\\n**Onboarding**:\\r\\nThe stage triggered by confirmed payment when a new student receives LSAT U\\r\\naccess and completes intake inside the platform. It does not require the person\\r\\nto reply to the consultation follow-up.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Consultation follow-up, application form, sales follow-up\\r\\n\\r\\n**LSAT U account creation**:\\r\\nCreating an account at `lsatprep.study`. A prospect may do this before payment,\\r\\nbut account creation alone does not grant student permissions or begin\\r\\nonboarding.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Onboarding, student access, enrollment\\r\\n\\r\\n### Relationship\\r\\n\\r\\nOne path, and everyone is on it. There is no separate category for a\\r\\nconsultation that never converted; that is a prospect who went quiet.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Prospect**:\\r\\nSomeone who has had a consultation but has never been taught a session.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Lead, inquiry, non-student\\r\\n\\r\\n**Active**:\\r\\nSomeone who is owed sessions, or who has been taught recently.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Current, enrolled, ongoing\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lapsed**:\\r\\nSomeone owed nothing who has gone quiet. Applies to a prospect who never\\r\\nconverted as much as to a student who stopped.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Churned, inactive, dormant, lost\\r\\n\\r\\n**Student record**:\\r\\nThe folder under `students/` holding everything authored about a person. A\\r\\nperson earns one by being **taught a session**, because teaching is what\\r\\nproduces notes, transcripts, and judgments the brain alone holds. A\\r\\nconsultation alone does not earn a record.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Profile, student file\\r\\n\\r\\n### Identity\\r\\n\\r\\n**Contact address**:\\r\\nThe address a student actually replies from, as evidenced in Gmail. The\\r\\nstudent page caches it; a booking pass merely records whatever was typed when\\r\\nthe pass was granted and is never authoritative for contact.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Email on file, registered email, account email\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage analytics URL**:\\r\\nThe shareable URL copied from a person\'s 7Sage Analytics page. Its canonical\\r\\nshape is `https://7sage.com/u/usr_<id>/review`.\\r\\n_Avoid_: 7Sage login-token URL, drill link, settings link, generic 7Sage URL\\r\\n\\r\\n### Notes on the model\\r\\n\\r\\n- **A student with no booking pass is the normal case, not a gap.** It means\\r\\n  nothing is owed to them; they book single sessions through their Cal.com\\r\\n  link. Only a student who is owed sessions should hold a pass.\\r\\n- The two paths are not exclusive. Someone can hold a pass from a package and\\r\\n  also have a Cal.com single-session link.\\r\\n- A **revoked** pass is a dead link and must never be treated as a way to book.\\r\\n  An **exhausted** pass is live but has no owed sessions left.\\r\\n- Status is authored, not computed: it is a judgment about the relationship.\\r\\n  But hard evidence that contradicts it gets surfaced rather than silently\\r\\n  tolerated, the same way a contact address that disagrees with the backend\\r\\n  does.\\r\\n- A prospect who never converts has no record, but is still tracked by name so\\r\\n  follow-up remains possible. Losing sight of them is the failure this guards\\r\\n  against.\\r\\n- **Arrangement and live status are different things and live apart.** What was\\r\\n  bought is authored and permanent; what remains is mirrored and regenerated. A\\r\\n  count that is typed by hand is the failure mode, not the feature: the\\r\\n  hand-written mirrors drifted within a day of being written.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Teaching after a session\\r\\n\\r\\n**Post-session lesson**:\\r\\nA student-facing teaching note grounded in a completed tutoring session, with worked reasoning and opportunities to check understanding. Joshua also calls it a session write-up, post-session recap, or session notes.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Chronological summary (that describes a different, narrower artifact)\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"964235","wall_time_seconds":0.2512982,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1697,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: reference\\r\\nsubject: Running Claude Code and Codex on one project the idea, and where ai-os stands\\r\\nupdated: 2026-09-22\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Running Claude Code and Codex on one project — the idea, and where ai-os stands\\r\\n\\r\\nDistilled from three clips: two ingested 2026-07-18 (archived at `raw/sources/clips/youre-using-claude-code-wrong-add-codex/` and `raw/sources/clips/codex-projects-in-5-mins/`) and the Herdr clip ingested 2026-09-22 at `raw/This Repo Just Solved The 1 Claude + Codex Headache.md`. These are method and architecture sources about the OS itself, not business or LSAT knowledge. Siblings of [[obsidian-command-center]] and [[second-brain-levels]].\\r\\n\\r\\n- **\\"You\'re Using Claude Code Wrong (Add Codex)\\"** — Chase AI, YouTube `VdxUKiF8CWI`, 2026-05-07. The *why + the tandem workflow*.\\r\\n- **\\"How to Use Your Claude Code Projects in Codex in 5 Mins\\"** — Nate Herk, YouTube `kB9iMD0EjT8`, 2026-05-18. The *how of file structure* — one project, both agents.\\r\\n- **\\"This Repo Just Solved The #1 Claude + Codex Headache\\"** — Chase AI, YouTube `neK8ydl0Vlk`, 2026-07-16. The *execution and observability layer* using Herdr.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The pitch: it\'s \\"and,\\" not \\"or\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nDon\'t pick Claude Code *or* Codex. Run both on the same project and let each check the other. The Venn diagram is ~99% overlap, so learning one gets you the other for free. The argument underneath is **tool agnosticism**: you owe these companies zero loyalty, models are converging, and pitting them against each other gets better output than staying tribal. Practical upside beyond quality: if one tool is down or stuck, you switch in seconds without losing context.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The tandem workflow (Chase)\\r\\n\\r\\nRun Codex in its desktop app (openai.com/codex) with a terminal open inside it, then run `claude` in that terminal, so both operate in the same directory. Then bounce work between them:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Plan review.** Codex plans → copy the plan into Claude (\\"what\'s missing, any blind spots?\\") → paste Claude\'s critique back → Codex revises. Two expert second-opinions before any code. Works in reverse too.\\r\\n- **Adversarial code review.** One builds, the other reads the same files and hunts bugs. In the demo Claude found ~20 real bugs on Codex\'s first pass. Telling the reviewer the *other* model wrote it may make it usefully more adversarial. There is a Codex plugin skill for Claude literally called \\"adversarial review.\\"\\r\\n- **Cost logic.** Uses more tokens up front, but catching bugs and weak plans early saves tokens in aggregate.\\r\\n- Desktop-app niceties Chase leans on: in-app browser + sidebar dev server, click-to-annotate/screenshot front-end reviews, diff viewer, \\"pets\\" background-activity indicator. Context note: GPT-5.5 Pro ~258k window vs Claude\'s ~1M; new chat = the `/clear` equivalent.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The file-structure map (Nate) — one project, both agents\\r\\n\\r\\nBoth agents read the **same shared knowledge** (docs, references, scripts, context files). Only the tool-specific wrappers differ:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Concern | Claude Code | Codex |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Instructions file | `CLAUDE.md` | `AGENTS.md` |\\r\\n| Config folder | `.claude/` | `.codex/` |\\r\\n| Skills folder | `.claude/skills/` | `.agents/skills/` |\\r\\n| Skill files | markdown + YAML front matter | **identical** markdown |\\r\\n| Agent files | markdown | **TOML** (same job, different format) |\\r\\n| Both support | global (`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`) + project-level scope | same |\\r\\n\\r\\n**3-layer mental model:** (1) shared knowledge any agent reads unchanged, (2) skills + agents — same content, just placed in each tool\'s folder, (3) tool-specific config that genuinely differs. Maintenance rule: a major change to `CLAUDE.md` gets mirrored to `AGENTS.md` (and skills/agents to their twin). Codex sub-agents don\'t auto-invoke — you call them explicitly.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Convert-any-project prompt (Nate\'s):**\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nI built this project using Claude Code, but I need you, Codex, to be able\\r\\nto use it too. Create an AGENTS.md that uses the CLAUDE.md as inspiration,\\r\\ncreate a .codex config, put all skills in .agents, put all agents in .codex\\r\\n(as TOML). Research both the Codex and Claude Code documentation and make\\r\\nsure everything important converts over.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Session handoff** (Nate\'s own skill) is the bridge: summarize decisions / active files / next steps, paste into the other tool, keep going. When one gets stuck, hand off and the other often unsticks it in seconds.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Herdr makes the tandem workflow visible and persistent\\r\\n\\r\\nHerdr is the terminal and session layer around Claude Code and Codex. It does not replace either agent or the shared-file architecture. It gives the workflow an operational surface:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Workspaces** separate projects.\\r\\n- **Tabs** separate activities within a project.\\r\\n- **Panes** show Claude Code, Codex, servers, tests, and helper agents side by side.\\r\\n- **Agent status** rolls up which pane is working, idle, or blocked.\\r\\n- **Persistence** keeps pane processes running after the Herdr client detaches or its terminal window closes. Reopen `herdr` to reconnect. Use `herdr server stop` only when the processes should actually stop.\\r\\n- **Agent control** lets an agent with the Herdr skill inspect neighboring panes, split panes, run commands, read output, and wait for another agent.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Installed setup on Joshua\'s Windows machine\\r\\n\\r\\nInstalled and verified on 2026-09-22:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Component | Installed state |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Herdr | Stable `0.9.1`; installed under `C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\.herdr\\\\packages\\\\standalone\\\\releases\\\\0.9.1-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; user PATH updated |\\r\\n| Claude Code | `2.1.280`; installed in the shared Node tools directory so Herdr panes can resolve it |\\r\\n| Codex CLI | `0.156.1`; installed in the shared Node tools directory so Herdr panes can resolve it |\\r\\n| Herdr integrations | Claude `v10` and Codex `v8`, both current |\\r\\n| Herdr agent skill | Installed globally for Claude Code and Codex |\\r\\n\\r\\nOpen a new PowerShell window so it receives the updated user PATH. Then:\\r\\n\\r\\n```powershell\\r\\ncd C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\r\\nherdr\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nInside Herdr, use the first pane for Claude Code:\\r\\n\\r\\n```powershell\\r\\nclaude\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nSplit right with the mouse menu or press `Ctrl+B`, release, then press `V`. In the new pane:\\r\\n\\r\\n```powershell\\r\\ncodex\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nRecommended layout:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Pane | Purpose |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Left | Claude Code as the primary operator or planner |\\r\\n| Right | Codex as reviewer, critic, or independent implementer |\\r\\n| Optional bottom pane | Dev server, tests, or logs |\\r\\n\\r\\nHerdr improves visibility but does not change the shared-worktree guardrail: Claude and Codex may read the same files, but they must not edit the same file at the same time. Assign explicit file ownership or use a worktree when both are implementing.\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst-run checks:\\r\\n\\r\\n```powershell\\r\\n"}'}]

New corrections are appended there as the next `E-NN`, written as the rule rather than the incident.\\r\\n- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don\'t over-hedge.\\r\\n- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Tools, keys, and secrets\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua\'s single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Joshua may hand a credential over in chat; take it and write it to `.env` without pushing back.\\r\\n- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Alex acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.\\r\\n- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.\\r\\n- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.\\r\\n- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.\\r\\n- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skills\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.\\r\\n\\r\\nActive in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Skill | Use it when |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua\'s explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |\\r\\n| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |\\r\\n| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |\\r\\n| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |\\r\\n| `consultation-inquiry-drafts` (email + DM channels), `consultation-followup-drafts` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |\\r\\n| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |\\r\\n| `session-tasks` | after a session: pulling out who owes what to which student by when, into `open-loops.md` + the task board. Joshua\'s to-do list, as distinct from `session-recap` (the student\'s document) and `wrapup` (the project ledger) |\\r\\n| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |\\r\\n| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |\\r\\n| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Vendored upstream skills.** `grilling`, `grill-with-docs` and `domain-modeling` are Matt Pocock\'s ([github.com/mattpocock/skills](https://github.com/mattpocock/skills), MIT), kept **byte-identical to upstream** so they refresh by overwrite. Never edit a vendored file: local changes go in a separate overlay skill that calls it, which is what `grill-me` is. Declared in `system/upstream-skills.json`; `system/scripts/check-upstream-skills.py` reports drift (`--diff` to see it, `--apply` to take upstream). **Updates arrive on their own**: the `com.joshua.ai-os.upstream-skills` launchd agent runs Mondays and Thursdays at 09:00 and records every change in `wiki/upstream-updates.md`. Twice-weekly because upstream ships in bursts (median 5 days between update days across its last 10). It cannot damage an overlay — `--apply` refuses to write to one, aborts if the manifest ever lists one as vendored, and re-verifies the overlay\'s declared `must_contain` additions in all six roots afterwards. Before assuming a vendored skill is current, read that ledger: the batch sat 7 weeks stale once and sent a session to the wrong skill.\\r\\n\\r\\n**`grill-with-docs` is model-invocable since 2026-09-01** (Joshua\'s call): the `disable-model-invocation` lock upstream ships was removed, so \\"grill with docs\\" in conversation fires it. It is an **overlay**, not vendored, so the auto-updater will not put the lock back. **Never substitute `grill-me`**: that is exactly the 2026-09-01 failure, where the skill was inactive, the model could not reach it, and silently improvised a wrong-format glossary into a project `CONTEXT.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When `/grill-with-docs` runs, confirm it actually loaded `grilling` and `domain-modeling`.** Upstream documents this as a real, unfixed rough edge: a skill that names another skill does not reliably cause it to load, and this one names two. The tell is a round that arrives with everything asked at once and no `➡️` recommendations attached, which is the model improvising an interview instead of running Matt\'s. Say so and load both explicitly rather than continuing. This is a second, independent way the skill can look like it ran and quietly do the wrong thing.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Codex runs the same set as Claude minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Guardrails\\r\\n\\r\\n- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.\\r\\n- **Root stays thin.** Don\'t accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.\\r\\n- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.\\r\\n- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\\r\\n- **Backtrack a bad retrieval, then fix the map.** When you miss something that was in the brain — you searched long for what should have been instant, or claimed you lacked access you had — do not just apologize and move on. Reconstruct where you actually looked, name where you should have looked, and then repair the cause: the routing line, the index entry, or the file\'s location. A retrieval failure is a defect in the map, not in the effort.\\r\\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\\r\\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\\r\\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\\r\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\r\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\r\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\"Remember that…\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\r\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.\\r\\n- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.\\r\\n- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.\\r\\n- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.\\r\\n- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal markdown under `.scratch/<feature>/`; this repo has no remote. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nSingle-context: `CONTEXT.md` + `docs/adr/` at the root. text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nimport sys,os,re,hashlib,json,collections\nfrom pathlib import Path\nsys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding='utf-8')\nroot=Path.cwd();src=Path(r'C:\\Users\\Joshu\\Documents\\Ai-os');report=root/'research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07';inv=json.loads((report/'source-inventory.generated.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))\ncurrent=[];sizes=collections.defaultdict(list);names=collections.defaultdict(list)\nfor d,ds,fs in os.walk(root,followlinks=False):\n ds[:]=[x for x in ds if not x.startswith('.') and x not in {'node_modules','__pycache__','venv','logs','cache','dist','build'} and not os.path.islink(os.path.join(d,x)) and not (Path(d)/x).is_relative_to(root/'raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07') and not (Path(d)/x).is_relative_to(report)]\n for name in fs:\n  p=Path(d)/name\n  if name.startswith('.') or p.is_symlink():continue\n  try:s=p.stat().st_size\n  except OSError:continue\n  sizes[s].append(p);names[name].append(p)\nrows=[];image_refs=[]\nfor f in inv['visible_files']:\n p=src/f['path'];same=root/f['path'];matches=[]\n for q in sizes[f['bytes']]:\n  if hashlib.sha256(q.read_bytes()).hexdigest()==f['sha256']:matches.append(q.relative_to(root).as_posix())\n rec=dict(f);rec['same_relative_path_exists']=same.exists();rec['same_relative_path_identical']=same.exists() and hashlib.sha256(same.read_bytes()).hexdigest()==f['sha256'];rec['exact_content_matches_in_current']=matches\n if p.suffix=='.md':\n  t=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8');local=re.findall(r'!\\[\\[([^\\]|]+)',t)\n  for link in local:\n   image_refs.append({'source':f['path'],'reference':link,'old_present':(src/link).exists(),'current_filename_matches':[q.relative_to(root).as_posix() for q in names[Path(link).name]]})\n rows.append(rec)\n(report/'comparison.generated.json').write_text(json.dumps({'scope':'Current visible files excluding preserved old archive and this report','files':rows,'embedded_local_images':image_refs},ensure_ascii=False,indent=2)+'\\n',encoding='utf-8')\nprint(json.dumps({'files_compared':len(rows),'same_path':sum(x['same_relative_path_exists'] for x in rows),'exact_content_matched_files':sum(bool(x['exact_content_matches_in_current']) for x in rows),'local_image_refs':len(image_refs),'local_image_missing_old_and_current':sum(not x['old_present'] and not x['current_filename_matches'] for x in image_refs)}))\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:500}));

Source timestamps and hidden-file hashes are recorded in the [generated inventory](../../../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/source-inventory.generated.json).\n+- Hidden configuration: five `.obsidian` JSON files were fully inspected and inventoried, but were not copied or activated. No current app configuration was changed.\n+- Transformations: none to archived source files. Current root instructions and live-system authority remain in force.\n+- Known missing attachments: 22 embedded local-image references have no matching file in either inspected local vault. External embedded images were not downloaded or visually inspected.\n+- Outputs: [comparison report](../../../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/report.md) and [source summary](../../../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md).\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/root-review.md\n+# Historical instructions and scratch files remain evidence\n+\n+Reviewed 2026-10-07. Every listed file was read in full. Sources remain byte-preserved in [the archive](../../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/PROVENANCE.md). The current vault's instructions and verified live systems remain authoritative.\n+\n+| Old file | Comparison and disposition |\n+|---|---|\n+| `AGENTS.md` | Historical Mac router with Alex identity, retired Codex/wiki restrictions, old app paths and no-remote assumptions. Current root instructions supersede these. Preserve as evidence; never activate. |\n+| `CLAUDE.md` | Historical Mac router and graph/tool configuration. Useful history, but it cannot establish installed Windows tools or current permissions. Preserve; never overwrite current instructions. |\n+| `GEMINI.md` | Historical Antigravity restrictions and append-only lesson from a past student-record edit. Current scoped instructions govern runtime behavior. Preserve as history; do not transplant restrictions. |\n+| `connections.md` | Dated 2026-07 operational snapshots, obsolete browser routes and legacy MCP claims. Current registry/live systems govern access. Preserve historical evidence without promoting access claims. |\n+| `CONTEXT.md` | Most glossary content already exists in current `CONTEXT.md`. Old version adds 7Sage baseline/update/profile terms and canonical profile frontmatter, but their pipelines are not verified here. Pricing differs: old says $45 for one 70-minute session, current says $45 per hour. Keep discrepancy explicit; no pricing replacement. Old native booking migration description is historical, not proof of live deployment. |\n+| `PLAN.md` | 2026-08 conditional cheat-sheet design plan. Current teaching curriculum has later work; preserve historical product reasoning, not current completion or publication state. |\n+| `PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md` | Conditional cheat-sheet review and triage. Useful quality lessons: contrapositive is not a second direction; examples must not invent terms; an indicator should not be claimed as transcript-taught without evidence. ## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e78dff","wall_time_seconds":0.2021644,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1437,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: reference\\r\\nsubject: The Claude Code Obsidian command center the idea, and where ai-os stands\\r\\nupdated: 2026-07-18\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# The Claude Code + Obsidian command center — the idea, and where ai-os stands\\r\\n\\r\\nDistilled from Chase AI\'s video \\"The Claude Code + Obsidian Setup That Now Runs My Life\\" (`raw/sources/clips/claude-code-obsidian-setup-runs-my-life/`, YouTube `glAoiBWVkmU`, 2026-05-15). Ingested 2026-07-18. This is a **method/architecture source about the OS itself**, not business or LSAT knowledge — the sibling of [[second-brain-levels]].\\r\\n\\r\\n## The pitch\\r\\n\\r\\nOpening Claude Code inside an Obsidian vault and using Obsidian only to read markdown \\"leaves a ton of value on the table.\\" Turn Obsidian into a **command center on top of Claude Code**: integrated terminal (drive Claude the way you always do) plus Obsidian\'s visual layer (dashboards, web viewer, file tree, calendar) at eye level. You keep the terminal; you add observability the terminal can\'t give.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The three moving parts\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Observability dashboard.** A custom Obsidian *plugin* (Claude Code wrote it — it is just code) rendering what you care about day-to-day: token burn, schedule, daily tasks, audience/metrics, research pulse (trending GitHub, Hacker News, AI headlines). Structure it however maps to your life — Chase uses overview / audience / research; others use one tab per business or per persona (owner hat vs dev hat). Design tip: prototype the look in Claude Design as if it were a web app, and ask for 3-5 style variants before committing.\\r\\n2. **Skill/automation shortcut buttons.** One-click launchers for the skills you already run (plan-today, morning brief, deep research, YT pipeline, weekly review). Each button opens a **headless** Claude (`claude -p`) so it runs without polluting your active session, usually emitting a report back into the vault that the dashboard then surfaces.\\r\\n3. **Free Obsidian ecosystem wins.** Community plugins: **terminal** (the integrated terminal), **hot reload** (edit the custom plugin without restarting Obsidian), **iconize** (folder icons). Core plugin: enable **web viewer** (off by default) so links open in-pane instead of kicking to Chrome.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The architecture point (file structure)\\r\\n\\r\\nAt scale (thousands of docs) you need a legible structure or Claude burns tokens hunting and you get pushed toward a full RAG system you didn\'t need. The reference is the **Karpathy structure**: `raw/` (unstructured input) → `wiki/` (internal Wikipedia pages distilling the raw) → `output/` (deliverables), with an **index file at every folder level** acting as a table of contents Claude reads on the way down. But the doctrine is: *you don\'t have to use those exact three.* Any structure works if a human can navigate it by name — \\"if you can explain it and someone can understand it, Claude probably can too.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n**CLAUDE.md: less is more.** At minimum it should hold (a) your vault structure so Claude knows where to go, (b) the navigation pattern for moving folder to folder, and (c) a short blurb on Obsidian best practices — wikilinks, embeds, tagging — so Claude writes files *in Obsidian format*. That is the symbiosis: Claude writes Obsidian-native, you read it natively.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cost note (dated, verify before quoting)\\r\\n\\r\\nPer the video, headless/programmatic `claude -p` use no longer draws from the Max subscription — Anthropic grants ~$200/month of separate credit that headless runs bill against at API rates (~10x Max-subsidized cost). Chase judges it a non-issue at real button-click volume (you still live in the terminal). Escape hatch: swap the headless layer to Codex headless — \\"5 minutes to refactor,\\" no output dip. **This is a 2026-05 claim about Anthropic billing; confirm current terms before relying on it.**\\r\\n\\r\\n## Where ai-os already stands\\r\\n\\r\\n| Video\'s idea | ai-os today |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Karpathy raw/wiki/output structure | Built and exceeded — `raw/` (immutable sources), `wiki/` + `corpus/` (two LLM-wikis), index-at-each-level, plus `students/` `decisions/` `context/`. See [[second-brain-levels]] (we sit at a complete Level 2). |\\r\\n| Thin CLAUDE.md = structure + navigation + Obsidian conventions | `CLAUDE.md` is a router with a routing-map table + retrieval order + a Voice section covering Obsidian-native writing. Richer than the video\'s minimum, same philosophy. |\\r\\n| Skills as the backbone, one-click launchers | Skill DB (`skills/INDEX.md`) with copy-to-activate model; **no dashboard buttons yet** — skills fire by `/name` in the terminal. |\\r\\n| Custom observability dashboard plugin | **Not built.** ai-os has `wiki/dashboard.md` (pointers, text) and `wiki/hot.md` (session cache), but no rendered visual plugin. |\\r\\n| Integrated terminal + web viewer + iconize + hot reload | Terminal plugin installed; `.obsidian/` shows `open-term`, `terminal-agent-tabs`, `obsidian-icon-folder`, `claude-sidebar`, `calendar`. Closest we are to the command-center surface. |\\r\\n| Headless `claude -p` button runs | Not wired. Skills run in-session. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## What\'s actually worth borrowing\\r\\n\\r\\nThe structure and CLAUDE.md philosophy we already match or beat. A project is not one level — each folder can sit at a different one.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Lvl | Question | Mechanism |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| 1 | Find by exact word/file | CLAUDE.md as router + `context/` `projects/` `decisions/` |\\r\\n| 2 | Pull everything on a topic together | LLM wiki — index + log + backlinks + hot cache + automemory + references |\\r\\n| 3 | I searched different words than I wrote (meaning) | Semantic search / vector DB, per-folder where it fits |\\r\\n| 4 | Trace a chain: topic X back to topic A | Knowledge graph — typed relationships (LightRAG, graphify) |\\r\\n| 5 | Never think about it | Always-on autonomous OS (GBrain, crons, constant sync) |\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo doctrines that outrank the levels:\\r\\n- **Context vs Connections.** Ingest *evergreen* data (locked decisions, quarterly priorities). Source author doesn\'t run L5. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Headline:** ai-os\'s Context/Connections separation is *more* formalized than the source\'s — Nate keeps the split in his head; ai-os has it written as `connections.md` (7 data domains, backend wins on conflict) + the \\"don\'t read the wiki unless the task needs it\\" rule + the Four Cs in `references/3ms-framework.md`. The video references the Four Cs as Nate\'s own framework; ai-os has already absorbed it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Recommendation\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not build Level 3, 4, or 5 now — no pain justifies it, and the doctrine warns against climbing levels for their own sake. A learner who cannot explain the logic may need fundamentals work; a learner who understands it but allocates time badly needs strategy practice. Labels are hypotheses to test with the learner, not facts inferred from a red answer alone.\\r\\n- **Read the whole timing sequence.** A missed final passage may result from earlier questions consuming the time. Correct earlier answers can still reveal costly inefficiency. Conversely, avoid treating late misses as proof that the final passage was intrinsically hardest.\\r\\n- **Second-pass review is distinct from blind review.** Revisiting a flagged item within the timed section should reconsider its conclusion, quantifiers and reasoning rather than automatically repeat the first interpretation. Its 90% confidence threshold and 60-to-120-second timing ranges are speaker-specific heuristics, not calibrated or adopted thresholds.\\r\\n- **Identify shared processes across question types.** Strengthen, weaken, evaluate, resolve and sufficient-assumption tasks can introduce information. Rejecting an answer merely because it was not stated in the stimulus may confuse these tasks with inference tasks. Necessary assumption is not simply strengthening, and sufficient assumption requires establishing the conclusion rather than making it somewhat more likely.\\r\\n- **Translate review into the next timed attempt.** The source asks learners to state the diagnosed issue before another section and ask how they could solve the question under timed conditions. This differs from achieving a correct answer only through unlimited rereading or dictionary use.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe RC discussion distinguishes explicit mention from implication and attributes a comparative-passage method and timing targets to the speaker. Priority explicitly admits it did not retrieve the complete catalog and grades 87 named plugins. Gemini describes broad coverage and several precise performance numbers without usable underlying citations. Both were created on 2026-08-23 and tailored to a 15,000-note, 8 GB Mac. Those assumptions do not establish this Windows laptop\'s present hardware, vault workload, or phone performance.\\r\\n\\r\\nConcrete conflicts to retain:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Priority recommends Tasks, cautious Tag Wrangler use, optional Excalidraw and an Omnisearch benchmark. Gemini rejects or strongly discourages them based on broad performance assertions.\\r\\n- Gemini asserts Native Bases uses SQLite, Omnisearch\'s index consumes hundreds of MB, Canvas Image LOD reduces 1.2 GB to 50 MB, and a final stack rests at 150-to-300 MB. These are unverified source claims, not established architecture or benchmarks.\\r\\n- Gemini\'s claim that local API token authentication ensures absolute data sovereignty does not establish how an external AI provider handles retrieved contents. Priority appropriately separates a local integration from provider-dependent transmission.\\r\\n- Both propose Local REST API with native MCP support. Its present API, release, endpoint, permission model and actual need must be verified before configuration. Filesystem access already available to local agents may cover much of the use case.\\r\\n- The overview explicitly retains Priority grades despite Gemini concerns and marks Canvas Image LOD\'s claimed performance numbers unverified. That synthesis is a research judgment, not Joshua\'s approval to install plugins.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe useful missing contribution is a **repeatable evaluation method**: baseline the actual device; add one capability at a time; compare startup, idle resource use, navigation and typing; keep a plugin only if a repeated real workflow improves. Prioritize native capabilities and avoid stacking duplicate indexes, sync layers or agent interfaces. A narrow read-only or new-test-note trial should precede bulk mutation. These are test-design ideas, not an instruction to change the present vault.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Only a few operator ideas need promotion\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Add the four context failure modes as a compact diagnostic aid: a wrong fact, too much irrelevant material, missing/misrouted context, or contradictory source generations. The source\'s categories overlap; use them to find evidence, not as a scoring system.\\r\\n2. When retrieval fails, record the requested fact, actual route taken, correct source, and narrow routing fix. Existing broad instructions to look first are not a replacement for tracing a specific failure.\\r\\n3. Preserve model-independent prompting lessons: outcome, purpose, acceptance criteria, real constraints, evidence-backed reporting and independent verification. Evaluate effort on representative tasks instead of copying benchmark or billing claims from video transcripts.\\r\\n4. Preserve the session-audit and skill-review process as possible improvements to existing operator workflows. Current `references/automation-backlog.md` already cites `brainstorms/2026-07-19-session-mining-reflection.md`; do not assume that process is missing solely because this video is absent.\\r\\n5. Keep Scrollcraft\'s visitor-belief/ordered-journey questions available for a future real website task. Existing design sources overlap; avoid inventing a new frontend doctrine or enabling paid asset calls from a transcript.\\r\\n\\r\\nAll old sources remain worth preserving, even where their lessons are represented. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"27e590","wall_time_seconds":0.3888927,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":268,"output":"corpus\\\\lr-explanations\\\\work\\\\codex-review\\\\round1.txt\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lr-explanations\\\\work\\\\codex-review\\\\BRIEF.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lr-explanations\\\\models\\\\Paradox-Method.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lr-explanations\\\\drafts\\\\batch-06-method-agree-part-b.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lr-explanations\\\\drafts\\\\batch-06-method-agree-part-a.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lr-explanations\\\\drafts\\\\batch-05-na-method-part-b.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lr-explanations\\\\drafts\\\\batch-05-na-method-part-a.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\Curriculum\\\\Comparison\\\\Homework\\\\Comparison homework and Review.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\Curriculum\\\\Necessary Assumption\\\\Notes\\\\_scan_Sabrina review.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\Curriculum\\\\Necessary Assumption\\\\Notes\\\\_scan_Sabrina ( review Nov9th).md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\Curriculum\\\\Method\\\\Homework\\\\Method and Flaw Strategy Guide.md\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lectures\\\\logical-reasoning\\\\must-be-true\\\\core\\\\2026-08-27-33013\\\\reader-preview.png\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lectures\\\\logical-reasoning\\\\must-be-true\\\\core\\\\2026-08-27-33013\\\\teaching-brief-preview.png\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lectures\\\\logical-reasoning\\\\must-be-true\\\\core\\\\2026-08-27-33013\\\\teacher-view-preview.png\\r\\ncorpus\\\\lectures\\\\logical-reasoning\\\\must-be-true\\\\core\\\\2026-08-27-33013\\\\teacher-socratic-preview.png\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"bffbd2","wall_time_seconds":0.2577114,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1287,"output":"# The Three Ms of AI™ — Mindset, Method, Machine\\r\\n\\r\\n> *Adapted from The Three Ms of AI™. © 2026 Nate Herk. All rights reserved.*\\r\\n> *The Three Ms of AI™ is a trademark of Nate Herk.*\\r\\n\\r\\n> *\\"The best automation is the one you barely notice. Start by eliminating what doesn\'t need to exist, then automate what\'s left with the least amount of AI possible.\\"*\\r\\n\\r\\n**Boring is Beautiful.**\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why this is in your kit\\r\\n\\r\\nThis framework is the operator brain you\'ll use every time you run `/level-up`. Three layers, each one builds on the last. Read it once, refer back as needed.\\r\\n\\r\\nHere\'s the thing most people get wrong: they think AI automation is about tools. It\'s not. Tools change every six months. The platform you\'re using today might not exist next year. What doesn\'t change is how you THINK about automation, how you DECIDE what to automate, and how you BUILD and OPERATE the thing once it\'s running. That\'s what The Three Ms of AI™ gives you. A way to think that works regardless of platform, model, or hype cycle.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis framework is for everyone. Business owners hearing about AI for the first time. Engineers exploring automation. Consultants who need a methodology they can hand to a client. It scales.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Layer 1 — MINDSET (How to Think)\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore you touch a single tool, you need to rewire how you approach work. The way you think about tasks determines whether you\'ll spot automation opportunities or walk past them every day.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. The Default Shift\\r\\n\\r\\nThe core habit: before doing any task the old way, ask \\"How could AI do this?\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the answer is \\"it can\'t do all of it,\\" the follow-up: \\"How could AI assist with the first 30%?\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nIt\'s never binary. The real question is always **\\"to what extent can AI be leveraged here?\\"** Maybe 80%. Maybe 10%. You don\'t know until you ask.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Real example.** Updating tracking links across 300+ YouTube video descriptions. The old way: open each video in YouTube Studio, find the link, replace it, save, next. Hours of mind-numbing work. The new way: describe the problem to Claude Code and walk to the kitchen for water. By the time you come back, it\'s researched the YouTube Data API, figured out quota limits, written a script, and laid out a plan. Approve, run, done. Now you have a reusable system.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Default Shift is like learning to type instead of writing by hand. Once it clicks, you physically cannot go back. Every manual task starts to itch.\\r\\n\\r\\n**One thing to internalize:** AI is better than you think, and improving faster than you think. Client needed infographic visuals; models couldn\'t deliver. Three months later, new model dropped. Shipped the work. *If AI can\'t do something today, try again next month. Seriously.*\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. The Function Breakdown\\r\\n\\r\\nYour role is a set of functions. Your job description has about five bullet points. Each breaks into dozens of tiny tasks. **You don\'t automate your whole job. You automate one tiny piece. Then another. Then chain them.**\\r\\n\\r\\nThink \\"automate a YouTube video.\\" Sounds impossible. Break it down: ideation, scripting, title generation, thumbnail generation, description writing, comment replies, timestamps, analytics. Each piece is its own automation. Build one, get it working, move on.\\r\\n\\r\\nOne small task per day. Six months later, hundreds automated. Compounding is real.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. The Curiosity Rule\\r\\n\\r\\nNever accept AI output without asking why. Ask for three alternatives. Ask which one it thinks is best and why. Push back. Dig in.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the antidote to \\"dark code\\" — automations or code you don\'t understand. **If you build something and you can\'t explain how it works, you\'ve built a liability, not an asset.** When it breaks (and it will), you\'ll have no idea where to start.\\r\\n\\r\\nTreat AI as a mentor, not a vending machine. The vending machine gives you output. The mentor gives you understanding.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Expect the Dip\\r\\n\\r\\nProductivity dip at the start: ~20% less output for the first week or two. New workflows, new prompting cadence. That\'s normal. Within two weeks, baseline doubles. But you have to push through.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Fail fast, learn faster.** Get to your first 10 mistakes as safely and quickly as possible. That\'s where the real learning lives, not in your first 10 successes.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Layer 2 — METHOD (How to Decide)\\r\\n\\r\\nMindset tells you how to think. Method tells you what to do with that thinking. The operational core — turning \\"I should probably automate something\\" into \\"here\'s exactly what I\'m building and why.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Find the Constraint\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo power questions surface everything:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q1:** *\\"If 500 new clients showed up tomorrow, what would break first?\\"* — finds bottlenecks (clogs in the pipe). Onboarding? Invoicing? Support response times?\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q2:** *\\"What would give you 500 more clients tomorrow?\\"* — finds growth opportunities (untapped pipe). Content you\'re not creating? Outreach you\'re not doing? Leads you\'re not following up on?\\r\\n\\r\\nOne finds what\'s broken. The other finds what could scale. Start with the constraint.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. EAD: Eliminate, Automate, Delegate\\r\\n\\r\\nFor every process, run EAD — in this order.\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/corpus/tutoring-diagnosis.md\n+---\n+type: synthesis\n+subject: Diagnose the cause of an LSAT miss before assigning work\n+created: 2026-10-07\n+updated: 2026-10-07\n+aliases: []\n+tags: [lsat, teaching-methods, review]\n+sources:\n+  - ../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md\n+---\n+\n+# Diagnose the cause of a miss before assigning work\n+\n+Private teacher reference recovered from Scott Milam and Christopher Nguyen's 7Sage tutoring webinar, dated 2022-01-11 in the preserved clip. This is an attributed coaching framework, not a new LSAT U scoring rule or a claim about a particular student. [Full source](../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/7Sage%20LSAT%20Tutoring%20Webinar.md); [comparison and limitations](../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/research-review.md).\n+\n+## A wrong answer can come from four different problems\n+\n+| Working hypothesis | What to investigate | What practice would address it |\n+|---|---|---|\n+| Reading | Can the student restate the relevant sentence, quantifier and relationship without changing them? | Accurate translation and a check against the exact wording. |\n+| Logical fundamentals | Can the student explain what follows, what does not follow, and why? | Targeted concept work and short worked examples. |\n+| Question process | Does the student know what this question asks an answer to do? | Contrast question tasks and explain why an answer meets this task. |\n+| Test-taking strategy | Does the student understand the reasoning after review but lose time or mismanage confidence under the clock? | Timed section review of skips, flags, revisits and opportunity cost. |\n+\n+These labels are hypotheses tested through the student's reasoning. An answer marked wrong does not reveal its cause on its own. A task that permits new information, such as Strengthen or Weaken, should not be judged by an inference task's requirement that the answer already follow from the stimulus.\n+\n+## Inspect the whole timing sequence\n+\n+A final-passage miss may begin with earlier questions consuming the available time. Review correct answers too: a correct but costly question can explain why the student never reached another question. Compare what the student did in the moment with what they can explain afterward before calling the final passage the problem.\n+\n+Flagging and second-pass review are different from later untimed blind review. On a timed return, inspect whether the student actually reconsidered the conclusion, scope or relevant relationship, or simply repeated the first reading. Rebuilding every flagged question from scratch is the speakers' suggestion; its opportunity cost still needs judgment.\n+\n+## Turn review into a change the student can test\n+\n+After review, name the specific failure and one behavior to test in the next timed attempt. “I found the right answer after unlimited rereading” is different evidence from “I can explain what I missed and how I will catch it under timed conditions.” Use the [Loophole method reference](loophole-online/index.md) when accurate translation or review practice addresses the diagnosed problem; keep the existing curriculum's logical standards.\n+\n+The webinar's numeric confidence thresholds, timing ranges, old difficulty ratings, historical score estimates and logic-games examples are source-specific historical material. They were not adopted as current policy. This page synthesizes the useful diagnostic distinctions, without publishing or assigning anything.\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/corpus/index.md\n@@\n ## Pages\n+\n+### Teaching methods recovered from the old local vault (2026-10-07)\n+\n+- [Loophole teacher reference](loophole-online/index.md): translation, quipping, CLIR, question strategies and review routines, with attributed source navigation and defective-capture warnings.\n+- [Diagnose the cause of a miss](tutoring-diagnosis.md): the recovered 7Sage four-category coaching framework and whole-section timing review, retained as a private teacher reference.\n+- [Source and comparison](../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md): preservation hashes, full local-file coverage, missing images and historical-policy conflicts.\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/references/obsidian-command-center.md\n@@\n ## What's actually worth borrowing\n+\n+### Recovered plugin research requires local testing\n+\n+The old vault contained three additional plugin-atlas reports, reviewed on 2026-10-07. Their recommendations disagree about Tasks, Omnisearch, Excalidraw and Tag Wrangler, and their catalog counts and performance numbers are not verified. Preserve them as [historical research](../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/research-review.md), with [source provenance](../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md).\n+\n+The useful method is to baseline the actual device, trial one capability at a time, compare startup, navigation and typing, and retain it only when a repeated workflow improves. A shortlist is not evidence that a plugin is installed or appropriate for this laptop or phone. No plugin or Sync setting was changed by this recovery.\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/references/second-brain-levels.md\n@@\n ## Recommendation\n+\n+### Diagnose a context failure before adding another retrieval layer\n+\n+The recovered “Steal My Exact AI OS Setup” clip offers four useful failure labels: a wrong fact entering context, too much irrelevant context, missing or misrouted context, and contradictory source generations. These categories overlap; use them to locate evidence, not as a new score or replacement for the current audit rubric. [Recovered-source review](../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/research-review.md); [provenance](../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md).\n+\n+For a failed lookup, record the requested fact, the route actually taken, the correct source, and the narrow routing change that would have prevented it. This makes “look before asking” actionable without assuming semantic search or a new plugin is the answer. The clip's architecture overlaps the existing router and live-system authority; no old runtime rule was activated.\n*** End Patch"));

Existing [Claude/Codex methods](../../references/claude-plus-codex.md), [Obsidian command-center ideas](../../references/obsidian-command-center.md), and [second-brain levels](../../references/second-brain-levels.md) remain their subject homes. Recovered tool/version/billing claims are dated source assertions, not verified current capabilities.\n+\n+The missing 7Sage coaching distinctions are compiled into [tutoring diagnosis](../../corpus/tutoring-diagnosis.md): reading, fundamentals, question process and timed strategy are separate hypotheses, and correct but costly questions can explain later misses. The Obsidian reference now records incremental plugin evaluation and conflicting reports; the second-brain reference now records the recovered context-failure taxonomy and route-tracing method. Both additions cite this batch rather than promoting historical product claims.\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/report.md\n@@\n - [Loophole teaching methods](../../../corpus/loophole-online/index.md) compile the recovered method library into an attributed teacher reference, with preserved-source navigation and defective-capture warnings.\n+- [Tutoring diagnosis](../../../corpus/tutoring-diagnosis.md) recovers the 7Sage four-category diagnosis and whole-section timing method. Existing [Obsidian command-center](../../../references/obsidian-command-center.md) and [second-brain levels](../../../references/second-brain-levels.md) references now include the useful plugin-evaluation and context-failure methods.\n*** End Patch"));

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"d87af6","wall_time_seconds":0.495628,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1956,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: synthesis\\r\\nsubject: Loophole Online teaching methods recovered from old Obsidian vault\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-07\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-07\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\ntags: [llm-wiki, lsat, teacher-research]\\r\\nsources:\\r\\n  - ../../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Loophole Online: teach retention, then diagnose the student\'s actions\\r\\n\\r\\nPrivate teacher research from Ellen Cassidy\'s Loophole Online clips captured 2026-08-24 through 2026-08-30. Publication dates are mostly absent. These are attributed source methods, not a newly approved LSAT U curriculum. The [source summary](../../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md) owns archive provenance and hashes. The [complete per-file review](../../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/teaching-review.md) links every recovered teaching file, including duplicate and defective captures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Make the student\'s understanding observable\\r\\n\\r\\nCassidy\'s core unit is **read, translate, quip (RTQ)** on a manageable chunk of text. Translation retells all conceptual information in the student\'s own words while looking away. A quip is a content-specific reaction, example, connection or question followed by an answer. It exposes whether the paraphrase represents understanding or merely recycled words. Preserve qualifiers, dates, lists and scope. The final analytical output is CLIR: controversy, loophole, inference or resolution according to stimulus structure. A final quip can already serve that role. [RTQ source](<../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/loophole-online/training-camp/translation/Whats Rtq • LO.md>), [translation source](<../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/loophole-online/training-camp/translation/Translate Prove You Were Listening • LO.md>)\\r\\n\\r\\nTeacher use: ask the student to retell a chunk and react to its exact content before accepting “I understood it.” Distinguish lost text from a bad inference. An educated vocabulary guess is provisional; ordinary context can help comprehension, but unsupported external facts do not become evidence for an answer.\\r\\n\\r\\nCassidy trains this process aloud with continuous recordings before silent use. Her foundation routine adds consecutive sections, vocabulary/common-knowledge reading and weekly marathons; its workload and timing goals are prescriptions from this source, not independently tested promises. [Foundation routine](<../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/loophole-online/routines/Translation Foundation Routine • LO.md>)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Choose a drill for the observed failure\\r\\n\\r\\n| Observed failure | Recovered source method |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Student forgets details after analyzing the stimulus | Proto-Stimulus Rebound: retell from memory, check missing/added/changed concepts. |\\r\\n| RC paragraphs disappear as reading continues | Decaf RC: full paragraph translations, then cumulative recaps of all preceding paragraphs. |\\r\\n| Abstract answer choices remain opaque | Three-column spreadsheet: exact choice, own-words translation, invented stimulus that would make it correct. |\\r\\n| Student mechanically diagrams before understanding | Personalized if-then paraphrase before diagramming; match both text and diagram to the paraphrase. |\\r\\n| Unless indicators remain slow or error-prone | Rotate three of four unless statements and diagram fresh, rather than memorizing diagrams. |\\r\\n| Student judges answers by familiarity or a single keyword | Read and translate the whole choice, then evaluate it against the actual stimulus and question task. |\\r\\n\\r\\nThese methods add concrete practice protocols to the existing [curriculum index](../index.md). Existing [conditional reference](<../Curriculum/Conditional Logic/Notes/_megadoc_reference_conditionals.md>) and [necessary-assumption notes](<../Curriculum/Necessary Assumption/Notes/_megadoc_notes_necessary_assumption.md>) remain their current authorities; no existing lessons were overwritten.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Review the process without revealing the key first\\r\\n\\r\\nCamo mixes missed questions with correct decoys and requires a fresh attempt before checking. Its four outcomes distinguish twice-wrong conceptual gaps, wrong-to-right misreads, right-to-wrong self-doubt and twice-right self-confidence. Those labels are diagnostic starting points; they do not establish the exact cause alone. Cassidy generally prescribes at least 24 hours before Camo and doing it after the day\'s new practice. **Accuracy Failsafe explicitly calls for immediate Camo after a five-minute break**, an exception the Camo workout allows. [Camo workout](<../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/loophole-online/workouts/Camo Review • LO.md>), [Failsafe workout](<../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/loophole-online/workouts/Accuracy Failsafe • LO.md>)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Wrong Answer Journal asks what the student did and what they will change, with brief transferable actions rather than long answer explanations. The Island holds questions still unclear after review for five-minute daily retries. [Journal](<../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/loophole-online/workouts/Wrong Answer Journal • LO.md>), [Island](<../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/loophole-online/training-camp/concept-review/How and Why to Use the Island • LO.md>)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Keep teaching caveats visible\\r\\n\\r\\n- Some necessary-assumption notes say that if the conclusion is true, the assumption must be true. That conflates a conclusion\'s truth with the support supplied by this argument. Use the existing argument-survival/negation treatment; retain these source formulations as unresolved, not adopted doctrine.\\r\\n- The necessary-condition trick generalizes a conditional principle-matching shortcut. Its unconditional rejection language needs adjudication before reuse, including equivalent contraposed formulations.\\r\\n- The Weaken clip asserts that exactly one answer can weaken at all. Preserve this as Cassidy\'s claim; do not turn it into a universal selection rule.\\r\\n- Cognitive mechanisms, speed stories, workload efficacy and historical tool/privacy claims are source assertions, not verified experiments or current product facts.\\r\\n- The two objection quizzes largely overlap; the chapter transcription has missing questions 1, 2 and 6. It complements the existing [Assumption versus Objection quiz](<../Curriculum/Flaw and Famous Flaws/Homework/Pop Quiz - Assumption vs Objection.md>), rather than replacing it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Navigate the complete recovered set\\r\\n\\r\\nThe [per-file review](../../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/teaching-review.md) provides a link and disposition for all **54 Loophole-tree files plus five related root/Clippings notes**.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Archived group | Files | Coverage |\\r\\n|---|---:|---|\\r\\n| Root README | 1 | Historical routing, stale inventory and a missing claimed corpus location. |\\r\\n| Routines | 1 | Translation Foundation. |\\r\\n| Accuracy | 1 | How to Camo. |\\r\\n| Concept review | 2 | Decaf RC and Island. |\\r\\n| Learn | 10 | Conditional, assumption, Method and Weaken clips. |\\r\\n| Translation | 25 | RTQ foundations, retention, reactions and worked examples. |\\r\\n| Workouts | 8 | Drill protocols. |\\r\\n| Duplicates | 5 | Three byte-identical pairs, two playback-time variants. |\\r\\n| Defective | 1 | Empty PT111.S3.Q14 file. |\\r\\n\\r\\nAdditional defects: PT111.S3.Q1/Q2 capture navigation without their demo transcripts; basic conditional diagramming and both NA lessons capture notes without video transcripts; PT111.S3.Q12 mixes a brakes demo with an unrelated pregnancy example. Root conditional practice embeds eight unavailable images. The misnamed “Just a Detail” clip contains a SQL migration, not a teaching lesson. No image or video contents were reconstructed from filenames, thumbnail links or expired media URLs.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e45191","wall_time_seconds":0.4992557,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1624,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: comparison\\r\\nsubject: Old Obsidian vault teaching source review\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-07\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-07\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\ntags: [llm-wiki, lsat, vault-migration]\\r\\nsources:\\r\\n  - ../../../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Old-vault teaching files: preserve evidence and use the actual lesson content\\r\\n\\r\\nReviewed the textual content of the 54-file Loophole tree and five assigned related notes. Large reads initially truncated; smaller follow-up reads recovered the meaningful content. Thumbnail URLs were omitted from a follow-up navigation-only read; no thumbnail/video pixels or missing local image attachments were interpreted. This is private teacher research. Originals were not edited.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What is actually new\\r\\n\\r\\nCurrent `corpus/loophole-online/` did not exist at review start, despite the old README claiming it held a synthesis. The corpus index lists no Loophole Online compilation. Targeted search of current corpus/wiki/raw Markdown found an archived Loophole marketing homepage, not these lesson transcripts. The existing Assumption-versus-Objection quiz is different content, and current conditional/NA pages share concepts without representing the complete recovered protocols.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe recovered clips are attributed to Ellen Cassidy/Elemental Prep through descriptions and lesson text, even where author frontmatter is empty. Original sources are preserved in each file\'s `source:` field under `https://new.theloophole.com/`. Captures range from 2026-08-24 through 2026-08-30; publication dates are generally unknown. No URLs were visited and no live platform claims were verified in this review. The [archive source summary](../../../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md) provides provenance/hash navigation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Every Loophole-tree file has a disposition\\r\\n\\r\\n“Missing/useful” means this recovered source is absent from the current source/compiled set and contains useful material. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4517c0","wall_time_seconds":0.2460805,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2188,"output":"---\\r\\ntitle: Students — one folder per student\\r\\ntype: operating-layer\\r\\ntags: [students, operating-layer]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-07-03\\r\\nupdated: 2026-09-25\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Students - one folder per student\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Students\\r\\n\\r\\nOne folder per student, holding **everything** about them (D2, locked 2026-07-16): transcripts, correspondence, homework, notes, schedules, profile. These are internal working records for Joshua, Claude, and Codex, not pages for students to open. Open the student\'s folder and their whole working record is there. This is one of the brain\'s three knowledge systems, beside [[../wiki/index|wiki]] and [[../corpus/index|corpus]].\\r\\n\\r\\n## Current students\\r\\n\\r\\n- [[adriana-varea/adriana-varea|Adriana Varea]] · [[amanda/amanda|Amanda]] · [[asia/asia|Asia]] · [[avneet-natt/avneet-natt|Avneet Natt]] · [[celia-cutler/celia-cutler|Celia Cutler]] · [[charles-moore/charles-moore|Charles Moore]] · [[dakota/dakota|Dakota]] · [[david-salgado/david-salgado|David Salgado]] · [[ethan/ethan|Ethan]] · [[favour/favour|Favour]] · [[fernande-charleus/fernande-charleus|Fernande Charleus]] · [[gianna/gianna|Gianna Jean]] · [[ivory-johnson/ivory-johnson|Ivory Johnson]] · [[nicole/nicole|Nicole]] · [[ore/ore|Ore]] · [[sandra/sandra|Sandra Kooh]] · [[sofia/sofia|Sofia]] · [[steven-morris/steven-morris|Steven Morris]] · [[venusa-laveaux/venusa-laveaux|Venusa Laveaux]] · [[vinay-kalva/vinay-kalva|Vinay Kalva]] · [[whit-wilson/whit-wilson|Whit Wilson]] · [[yahunatan-zetvy/yahunatan-zetvy|Yahunatan Zetvy]] · [[zeina-borji/zeina-borji|Zeina Borji]]\\r\\n\\r\\n**Taught in the last month** (2026-08-08 to 2026-09-08, transcripts pulled 2026-09-08): Celia Cutler (6), Adriana Varea (5), Steven Morris (4), Sofia (2), Vinay Kalva (2), Yahunatan Zetvy (2), Asia (1), Charles Moore (1), Fernande Charleus (1), Whit Wilson (1).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Former students\\r\\n\\r\\nLive in [[non-students/CLAUDE|non-students/]] as of 2026-09-10. Records preserved, excluded from every sweep. Read that folder\'s `CLAUDE.md` before touching anything in it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The primary/mirror boundary (D4 — read before regenerating anything)\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery file in a student folder is one of two kinds, and the distinction decides what a rebuild may overwrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Primary — the brain is the only home.** Transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, study plans, anything Joshua or an agent authored about the student. **Never regenerate, never overwrite; retain forever.**\\r\\n- **Mirror — a projection of a live backend.** Session/booking state (Cal.com + Google Calendar), homework/dossier/publish state (LSAT U Supabase), payment events (Stripe), correspondence indexes (Gmail). On any disagreement the backend wins, silently.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Convention:** mirror files MUST be named `*.generated.md` (or carry `generated: true` frontmatter). A file without that marker is primary by definition and must be treated as precious. Pre-2026-07-16 files (`email-context.md`, `payment-links.md`, `transcripts.md`) predate the marker — treat as primary until individually re-derived and renamed. No Markdown file here is ever the source of operational truth — no package balances, no running totals (all services are paid upfront; Cal.com answers sessions-used).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Standard files per student folder\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the target layout, not a checklist to satisfy with empty files. Create a record when its source or a real teaching judgment supports it. Leave an absent record absent until there is verified content to put in it.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `<student-slug>.md` — internal entry page and folder router (primary). Its `## Open this record` block links only to local files that exist.\\r\\n- `email-context.md` — synthesized Gmail context.\\r\\n- `email-correspondence.md` — Gmail message-id corpus for the student\'s confirmed address.\\r\\n- `payment-links.md` — verified payment, billing, and scheduling links.\\r\\n- `transcripts.md` — transcript and meeting-note inventory. Its `## Local session files` table links directly to every full local Markdown export and every session package, newest first. A Google Doc link is provenance, never the only way to read a session.\\r\\n- `teaching-notes.md` — durable, evidence-backed learning judgments and teaching patterns (primary).\\r\\n- `open-loops.md` — unfinished follow-ups and pending decisions (primary).\\r\\n- `live-status.generated.md` — currently a refreshable LSAT U booking-pass mirror. Calendar, Gmail, and payment state remain in their live systems; this file does not yet summarize them.\\r\\n- `transcript-downloads/` — preserved local exports of Gemini notes (source evidence; do not hand-edit). A raw export here is not itself a completed session package.\\r\\n- `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` — new self-contained session packages.\\r\\n- `assignment-log.md` — running log of assigned work and notification evidence so repeats are avoided (primary).\\r\\n- `Practice_Test_Reports/` — the student\'s PT score reports as received (intake form, screenshots, exports). Raw evidence — read for diagnosis, never edit.\\r\\n- `Student_Specific_Homework/` — work assigned to this student specifically. Distinct from the shared banks in `corpus/Curriculum/*/Homework/`, which are the source material it draws from.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- New student → new folder `students/<slug>/` with a `<slug>.md` profile page.\\r\\n- A consultation followed by a subsequent student session establishes onboarding. Check the Calendar sequence and identity before creating the folder; record any uncertainty about attendance or contact aliases. Consultation-only records stay in `_other-sessions/`.\\r\\n- When Joshua explicitly says a student relationship is over or to close a folder, move the preserved record to `students/non-students/<slug>/`, remove it from the current student index, and refresh the generated roster. Do not delete the record or silently infer closure from inactivity.\\r\\n- New session → `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` containing `transcript.md`, `summary.md`, and `topic-map.md`.\\r\\n- Keep the original full Gemini export as a local Markdown file in `transcript-downloads/` when available. Build the session package from verified session evidence, and link both the export and package from `transcripts.md`; older exports may predate the package convention.\\r\\n- `transcript.md` preserves the raw source. `summary.md` records the auto-saved summary, action items, and appended corrections. `topic-map.md` is a timestamped link index to relevant LSAT concepts, student records, and source material.\\r\\n- Session summaries are append-only. Later corrections go in a dated correction section and never erase the original summary.\\r\\n- Generated operational state is clearly marked with `generated: true` and never overrides primary teaching records.\\r\\n- Wikilink student pages into corpus concepts and LSAT U material as they become relevant.\\r\\n- Tier A note: transcripts and correspondence are trackable (Tier-A amendment, 2026-07-16). True secrets (credentials, IDs, payment instruments) still never enter Git — `private/` only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Internal routing contract after each session\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Match the Calendar event and Gemini Doc by guest email, date, and content. Preserve the complete source export in `transcript-downloads/`; never rely on a Drive link as the record.\\r\\n2. Create or update the session\'s `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` package without replacing a primary transcript or summary. Record any source mismatch as a dated correction.\\r\\n3. Add a newest-first row to `transcripts.md` under `## Local session files`: date, direct local full-export link, local package links, and source/status. Include raw-only historical exports and packages even when the other half is absent.\\r\\n4. Update evidence-backed `teaching-notes.md` and `open-loops.md`. Refresh the student\'s `<slug>.md` `## Open this record` block so Joshua, Claude, and Codex can reach the latest work in two clicks. Link only to files that exist.\\r\\n5. Check that all local links resolve, that the full Markdown export contains the transcript rather than only Gemini\'s summary, and that the session index has no unlinked export or package. Leave attendance and missing-source uncertainty explicit.\\r\\n6. Run `python system/scripts/check-student-records.py` from the repo root before calling intake complete. It checks all student routers and every local Markdown export and session package link.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Operating contract\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each new consultation or transcript, Jay 2 must report what was found, what was created or updated, what was inferred, what remains missing, what is recommended, and what Joshua should confirm or reject. During the learning period, no student-facing message, assignment, or platform action is implied by record preparation alone.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"04bd6b","wall_time_seconds":0.2442169,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1507,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: notes\\r\\ntopic: necessary_assumption\\r\\ncluster_type: duplicate_pile\\r\\nsource_count: 10\\r\\ntotal_words: 4583\\r\\ngenerated_at: 2026-04-24T08:09:23.979986+00:00\\r\\nsources:\\r\\n  -
