text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/corpus/Curriculum/Reading Comprehension/Notes/cumulative-translation-review.md\n+---\n+type: teaching-notes\n+subject: Check whether earlier RC paragraphs survive later reading\n+created: 2026-10-07\n+updated: 2026-10-07\n+aliases: []\n+tags: [lsat, reading-comprehension, teacher-reference]\n+sources:\n+  - ../../../../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md\n+---\n+\n+# Check whether earlier paragraphs survive later reading\n+\n+Private teacher method recovered from Ellen Cassidy's Decaf RC workout, captured 2026-08-27. Use it when observed recall loses earlier details as a passage continues. This is a practice protocol, not a universal timed-test prescription or evidence that any particular student needs it.\n+\n+## Check recall cumulatively\n+\n+Have the student read a paragraph, restate its information in their own words and react to its specific content. Looking away, they retell that paragraph. After the second paragraph, they retell it and then the first two together. Continue the cumulative retelling as later paragraphs are added.\n+\n+The source's target is a full translation, including relevant names, dates, lists and qualifiers. A gist or main point is a different output. Keep that distinction explicit when checking the exercise. Success on a gist task does not establish success on a detailed-recall task.\n+\n+Start aloud so the teacher can hear omissions, additions and changed relationships. Check those against the passage, and record the actual discrepancy. The source later permits silent practice, but silent confidence alone does not show the teacher what was retained.\n+\n+## Choose it for an observed reading problem\n+\n+Separate losing the passage from misunderstanding a question task or running out of time earlier in the section. The [tutoring diagnosis framework](../../../tutoring-diagnosis.md) explains those distinct hypotheses. For LR recall after analysis, the related Proto-Stimulus Rebound protocol retells the stimulus after CLIR, checks missing/added/changed information, and records the discrepancy; see the [method collection](../../../loophole-online/index.md).\n+\n+When this method addresses an actual student observation, record the evidence in that student's teaching notes and link this shared page. Do not duplicate the entire drill or infer a learning problem merely because this source recommends it. [Student-record conventions](../../../../students/README.md).\n+\n+Cassidy's stated workload, 12-minute aloud/8-minute silent goals and advice for 70-minute accommodations are historical vendor recommendations. They were not adopted as Joshua's timing targets or assigned to anyone. Follow [Joshua's teaching voice standard](../../../../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) when turning evidence from this method into a student-facing explanation.\n+\n+[Preserved Decaf workout](<../../../../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/loophole-online/workouts/Decaf RC Translation Drill • LO.md>); [source summary](../../../../wiki/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07.md); [corpus index](../../../index.md).\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/corpus/Curriculum/Conditional Logic/Notes/_megadoc_reference_conditionals.md\n@@\n Conditional Intro answer key.gdoc\n+\n+## Teacher references connected on 2026-10-07\n+\n+The recovered Loophole clip recommends checking an ordinary if-then paraphrase before accepting a diagram. This idea is already developed in [Joshua's selected teaching standard](../../../../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md#8-a-translation-is-checked-by-trying-both-interpretations), so it does not need a competing conditional lesson. The [recovered method reference](../../../loophole-online/index.md) supplies the attributed clip and its limitations. Preserve Joshua's five-step method and the existing indicator groups when using that check.\n+\n+Use the [tutoring diagnosis framework](../../../tutoring-diagnosis.md) to distinguish inaccurate reading from incorrect diagram placement or an unsupported inference, based on what the student actually explains. Source recovery does not itself diagnose a student.\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/corpus/Curriculum/Flaw and Famous Flaws/Homework/Pop Quiz - Assumption vs Objection.md\n@@\n # Pop Quiz: Assumption vs. Objection\n+\n+> **Private teacher reference, added 2026-10-07:** The older vault contains an additional objection quiz with different examples. This page keeps the vendor-source comparison and cross-topic method overview in one place; the topic pages remain the teaching entry points. Keep `ai-os` as one working Obsidian vault, with the [wiki](../wiki/index.md), [teaching corpus](../corpus/index.md) and [student records](../students/README.md) as distinct knowledge domains. This preserves the existing architecture; it does not authorize a folder move, vault split or Sync change.\n+\n+The useful unit is a connected reasoning chain. A student's session evidence supports a dated teaching judgment in their record. That judgment links to a reusable concept or method in the corpus. Its student-facing explanation uses [Joshua's selected teaching voice](lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md). Product or pedagogy choices link to the [decision log](../decisions/log.md), while sources remain evidence with provenance. [Business context](../context/work.md) and [Joshua's identity](../context/about-me.md) explain the purpose and preferences behind the work. Keeping those links in one vault makes the whole chain available without copying each shared lesson into every student folder.\n+\n+Separate vaults would require additional cross-vault retrieval and synchronization conventions. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"d1c7e2","wall_time_seconds":0.2410066,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3345,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 3345)\\nTotal output lines: 65\\n\\n# Conditional opening: the reference now sets the sequence\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2026-10-03 first-ten-minute clip supplements the opening\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua asked to incorporate the newly clipped LSAT Lab material through about minute ten, focusing on Groups 1 and 2. The [bounded source and twelve-image review](../../wiki/sources/lsat-lab-conditional-logic-clip.md) is the evidence record. All embedded screenshots were examined and preserved with hashes. Its language-recognition section at 7:58–10:18 enriches sections 3, 5, 8 and 11 below: certainty across language forms; ordinary requirement readback; full attached ideas; sentence placement versus diagram placement; equivalent expressions; and requirement meaning beyond a memorized list.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source\'s keyword board groups rules/universals on the left and guarantees/requirements on the right. Do not treat the entire vendor list as Joshua\'s simple Group 1/2 inventory: no and unless/until/without require later negative/exception mechanics. Only versus the only, always, will and must need grammatical and semantic context. Keep the source\'s earlier compound-negation and linking content for the corresponding later concepts already in the curriculum. The thirteen-part user-selected sequence below remains the skeleton; the video does not override it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Current skeleton selected on 2026-10-03\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua\'s latest instruction makes the supplied Conditional Reasoning HTML the **content-order backbone**, not just a voice reference. Every supplied conceptual beat must have a home in the same relative order. Original wording, original examples, deeper explanations and well-placed interactions are encouraged. This supersedes the earlier opening-only scope and sequence recorded below. The local implementation now renders this order inside the existing orientation lesson; historical assignments retain their identities.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe OneDrive file `C:/Users/Joshu/OneDrive/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html` was read in full and matches the [archived source](../../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html) byte for byte: SHA-256 `13afbb1ecd2d4b0f80ca918d1956690e16493b1addbf273314c9c1124ad380cb`. [Voice and source limits](../../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) still apply. No new source generation is needed.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source starts with the if/then relationship; it does not contain a separate condition-definition chapter. Joshua\'s requested brief definition of a condition and plain LSAT purpose belong at the start of that opening. They should prepare the source\'s first idea, not create a long detour.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The complete teaching timeline\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are authoring sections, not difficulty levels, assignment labels, or a student-facing catalogue. Each can contain several short notes and interactions. Joshua assigns the appropriate lesson or practice; a student sees that assigned work.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Order | What we teach, in sequence | What the learner does here |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **1. What a conditional tells you** | Define a condition as an idea that can be true or false. Define a conditional through if/then. Explain what the relationship lets us know and why that matters for LSAT conclusions. Read one original rule naturally. Explain that must rules out an exception to the stated relationship. Distinguish imagining the if-condition from reporting that it occurred. | Read the rule; describe what would follow **if** its first condition were true. A brief meaning check comes before notation. |\\r\\n| **2. Sufficient: enough to establish the other condition** | Name the if-part. Explain that it may or may not be true. If it is absent, this rule alone does not settle the other condition. If it is present, the required condition follows. Define using or activating the rule through that present sufficient condition. | Compare the same rule with a sufficient-present fact and a sufficient-absent fact. Explain both conclusions before revealing the answers. |\\r\\n| **3. The sufficient word family** | Introduce the family in the source\'s row order: if; when/whenever; any/anytime; all; every/every time; in order to; people who; each. Show equivalent ways to express one relationship. Explain grouped forms and optional endings. Explain why understanding the sentence matters more than spotting a word. | Compare ordinary-English versions of a stable relationship; identify the complete sufficient idea. No arrow-construction task yet. Add current Group 1 expressions as clearly contextual extensions here. |\\r\\n| **4. Necessary: what has to be true** | Name the then-part. Explain its required role when the sufficient condition is true. Establish certainty about that requirement. Avoid implying that the condition must happen later in time. | In the same anchor, explain what is required and why. Keep the sufficient and necessary roles visible as complete ideas. |\\r\\n| **5. The necessary word family** | Follow the source\'s row order: then; must; necessary; required; only/only if; depends; need/need to; have to; essential; precondition. Show how these express the same relationship as the earlier family. Give only if special attention: it identifies a requirement despite containing if. Explain that symbols will help keep the relationship clear when wording changes. | Read and compare necessary-language sentences, including a controlled if versus only-if contrast. Add current Group 2 forms here without silently reclassifying Joshua\'s groups. |\\r\\n| **6. Discover the contrapositive** | Restate the sufficient-to-necessary relationship. Use a fresh thought experiment. Remove the necessary condition and ask whether the sufficient condition could still be true. Pause. Derive the answer from the unmet requirement. Name the contrapositive only after that reasoning, then return to the anchor. | Predict before reveal, explain the contradiction in ordinary English, and apply the reasoning to the anchor. Do not start with flip-and-negate. |\\r\\n| **7. Compare all four situations** | Te…345 tokens truncated… contrapositive from reading it when useful. | Build and read each form; explain why the transformed rule says the same thing. Teach Joshua\'s full five-step routine here: indicator; two complete ideas; role placement; contrapositive; English readback. |\\r\\n| **10. Answer practical diagramming questions** | Keep the source\'s FAQ order: when to diagram; how to check correctness and build confidence; concern about time; when to write a contrapositive; why training still asks for written contrapositives; how to choose readable abbreviations. | Compare a diagram with the original sentence. Diagnose a specific mistranslation and record what caused it. Start untimed; timing can be added to teacher-assigned medium/advanced practice. |\\r\\n| **11. Find the indicator\'s target** | Define target. Mark complete targets in familiar sentences. First show the usual position after an indicator. Then show targets before an indicator. Then show the same indicator moving relative to its target. The source\'s obscured line remains missing evidence, not a sentence to reconstruct. | Highlight the complete idea, including qualifications; compare meaning-preserving word-order variants. The closest noun or next word is not an automatic target rule. |\\r\\n| **12. Check with Personalized If/Then** | Define the translation check and why it helps. Read ordinary meaning; propose the sufficient condition; imagine a case meeting it; ask what must be true. Work a correct interpretation, then a deliberately reversed interpretation and explain why the latter fails. Finish by recommending the meaning check. | Perform both readings against the same whole sentence. Keep who or what the sentence concerns intact. Extend with Joshua\'s What Test as a complementary meaning check. |\\r\\n| **13. Practice the whole process** | Explain the drill\'s purpose, permit looking back, and require an original diagram and contrapositive. Begin with straightforward sufficient language, then varied sentence placement and necessary language. The source\'s six items model a basic translation set, not complete family coverage. | Attempt before answers. Use fresh original items and explain the target, roles, original, contrapositive and English readback. Provide both targeted family practice and a mixed transfer set. |\\r\\n\\r\\n### An original anchor for the rebuild\\r\\n\\r\\n**If an order is marked ready for pickup, it has been packed.**\\r\\n\\r\\n- Opening: imagine an order with the ready label. Packing follows under the stated rule; the sentence hasn\'t said an actual order has the label.\\r\\n- Sufficient: a ready label is enough to establish packing. Without the label, packing remains possible.\\r\\n- Necessary: packing is required for an order carrying that label.\\r\\n- Contrapositive: an order that hasn\'t been packed cannot be marked ready under this rule. Let the student work this out before naming it.\\r\\n- Four situations: ready implies packed; not packed implies not ready; not ready leaves packing open; packed leaves the ready label open.\\r\\n- Notation, introduced later: Ready → Packed; Not packed → Not ready. Abbreviate only after defining the complete labels.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis anchor is our own course example. Reuse it when a new view deepens the idea, and use fresh situations for transfer. A statement such as a published report requiring editor approval can provide a separate contrapositive prediction: no editor approval means it cannot be published under the rule. Do not treat an obligation or permission as proof that someone complied or acted.\\r\\n\\r\\n### How the current material fits\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep useful material, relocate it to the concept it trains, and add missing depth. text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nimport re,json,os,collections\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom urllib.parse import unquote\nr=Path(r'C:\\Users\\Joshu\\Documents\\Ai-os');md=list(r.rglob('*.md'));names=collections.defaultdict(list)\nfor p in md:names[p.stem].append(p)\nrefs=[]\nfor p in md:\n t=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\n for m in re.finditer(r'(!?)\\[\\[([^\\]]+)\\]\\]',t):\n  target=m.group(2).split('|')[0].split('#')[0].strip()\n  if not target:continue\n  paths=[p.parent/target,r/target,p.parent/(target+'.md'),r/(target+'.md')];hits=[str(q.relative_to(r)) for q in paths if q.exists() and q.is_relative_to(r)]\n  if not hits and '/' not in target and '\\\\' not in target:hits=[str(q.relative_to(r)) for q in names[Path(target).stem]]\n  refs.append({'source':p.relative_to(r).as_posix(),'target':target,'kind':'embed' if m.group(1) else 'wikilink','resolved':bool(hits),'matches':sorted(set(hits))})\nresult={'old_markdown_files':len(md),'wikilink_occurrences':sum(x['kind']=='wikilink' for x in refs),'wikilink_locally_resolved':sum(x['kind']=='wikilink' and x['resolved'] for x in refs),'wikilink_unresolved':sum(x['kind']=='wikilink' and not x['resolved'] for x in refs),'embed_occurrences':sum(x['kind']=='embed' for x in refs),'embed_resolved':sum(x['kind']=='embed' and x['resolved'] for x in refs),'scope':'Old local Obsidian wikilink syntax only; not external URLs, Markdown links, graph relationships or source claims','references':refs}\np=Path('research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/old-links.generated.json');assert not p.exists();p.write_text(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2)+'\\n',encoding='utf-8');print(json.dumps({k:v for k,v in result.items() if k!='references'}))\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:700}));

## assistant

Updated [the existing comparison report](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/report.md) with:

- 20 document-family mappings comparing actual meanings and approaches.
- Exact old/current top-folder and Markdown counts.
- Old strengths, current strengths and remaining weaknesses.
- Link/configuration comparison and explicit inspection limits.

The old vault is stronger as a compact source library with detailed transcripts and drills. Its actual contents are **29 root files plus 64 clips**, not the full operating system its routers describe.

The current vault has real student, curriculum, context and decision records. Its total Markdown count exaggerates usable knowledge: **823 files in wiki/students/corpus**, versus thousands of skills, graph imports and exported conversations.

Existing conditional methods, Herdr and Claudex largely overlap. Loophole protocols, the explicit 7Sage diagnostic framework and RC retention method were missing before this intake. Historical pricing/tool claims remain unresolved.

Checked 125 report links and 186 source/archive hashes, zero errors. Only the report and two generated comparison inventories changed. Full old-vault inspection is verified; exhaustive semantic reading of every current file is not claimed.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"876545","wall_time_seconds":0.5322265,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1352,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: reference\\r\\nsubject: Graphify to Obsidian turning a corpus into a navigable map\\r\\nupdated: 2026-07-20\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Graphify to Obsidian: turning a corpus into a navigable map\\r\\n\\r\\nHow to take any corpus, build a knowledge graph from it, and land that graph in\\r\\nthe vault as linked markdown Claude can traverse. Method note, not state.\\r\\n\\r\\nSource: Chase AI, \\"Graphify + Obsidian + Claude Code = CHEAT CODE\\" (2026-06-08),\\r\\nclipped 2026-07-20, ingested the same day. First run: `graph-imports/claude-code-docs/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why it exists\\r\\n\\r\\nGraphify alone produces a graph in a vacuum. It maps one corpus and knows nothing\\r\\nabout anything else. The vault is the opposite: wide context, no concept map. The\\r\\nbridge writes every graph node out as a markdown note with backlinks matching the\\r\\ngraph edges, so the map lands inside the context.\\r\\n\\r\\nWorth it when the corpus needs to sit next to material already reasoned about.\\r\\nNot worth it when the corpus is self-contained.\\r\\n\\r\\n## How it works\\r\\n\\r\\n`--obsidian` (with `--obsidian-dir <path>`) is a real flag on the graphify skill\\r\\npipeline, step 6. Not a bare CLI subcommand: `graphify --help` does not list it.\\r\\n\\r\\nNodes are **concepts, not documents**. The first run pulled 170 doc pages into 963\\r\\nconcepts and 1,239 connections. That ratio is the point. One note per source page\\r\\nwould just be a copy; concepts plus edges are a map.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The four integration strategies\\r\\n\\r\\nRanked by how easily undone:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Standalone vault** - graphify\'s default (`graphify-out/obsidian`). Fully siloed.\\r\\n2. **Quarantine subfolder** - write into the main vault under one folder. Delete\\r\\n   the folder to undo. What we used.\\r\\n3. **Selective harvest** - keep only the nodes worth keeping.\\r\\n4. **Redistribution** - scatter notes into existing folders. Best coherence,\\r\\n   hardest to reverse.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The step that actually matters\\r\\n\\r\\n**Generated stubs are near-worthless on their own.** A stub is a title, a list of\\r\\nedges, and a `source_file:` string in frontmatter. That string is inert text: the\\r\\nsource document is not in the vault, so there is nothing to click and nothing for\\r\\nClaude to read.\\r\\n\\r\\nWiring nodes to their sources is what converts the stub from a dead end into a\\r\\nsignpost. Copy the sources into the vault and give every concept note a `## Source`\\r\\nsection linking to the full document. Skipping this produces a graph that looks\\r\\nimpressive and answers nothing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Fragmentation is the real failure mode\\r\\n\\r\\nNot mentioned in the source, found on the first run, and it would have quietly\\r\\ngutted the result.\\r\\n\\r\\nExtraction runs as parallel subagents over disjoint file chunks. Each agent only\\r\\nsees its own chunk, so it only ever writes edges *within* that chunk. The naive\\r\\nmerge produced **27 disconnected components, the largest holding 147 of 986 nodes**.\\r\\nA graph in 27 pieces cannot answer a cross-document question, which is the entire\\r\\nreason to build one.\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo fixes, in order:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Merge duplicate-label nodes.** IDs are `{file_stem}_{entity}`, so the same\\r\\n   concept in two files gets two IDs. Normalize labels and collapse. Took 27\\r\\n   components to 18 and the giant component to 92%.\\r\\n2. **Run a stitching pass.** Feed one agent the orphan components plus the main\\r\\n   component\'s hubs and ask for bridging edges. 72 edges reached 100% connectivity.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Always check `nx.connected_components` before shipping a graph.** A single\\r\\ncomponent near 100% is the health signal. Do not trust node and edge totals alone;\\r\\nthey look identical whether the graph is connected or in pieces.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Other traps\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Give extraction agents explicit file lists, never an index into a manifest.**\\r\\n  Found on the vault run. Handing each agent \\"take element at index 8 (chunk 9 of\\r\\n  14)\\" saved prompt size and cost a whole chunk: one agent resolved the phrasing to\\r\\n  index 9, re-extracted its neighbour\'s files, and left its own 22 files unread.\\r\\n  Node and edge totals looked healthy throughout. Only the per-file coverage check\\r\\n  caught it. Paste the paths.\\r\\n- **Docs move.** The video\'s `docs.claude.com` path is dead; Claude Code docs are at\\r\\n  `code.claude.com/docs/llms.txt`. Look for `llms.txt` before scraping.\\r\\n- **Subagent deaths are not always fatal.** Five of eight agents reported a session\\r\\n  limit, but all five had already written their chunk files. Check disk before\\r\\n  re-running: `.graphify_chunk_*.json` existing and parsing is the success signal.\\r\\n- **`to_json` refuses to shrink a graph.** Legitimate dedupe trips its guard; pass\\r\\n  `force=True`.\\r\\n- **Validate agent-produced node IDs** against the real ID set before merging edges,\\r\\n  and drop dangling ones.\\r\\n- **Verify coverage** - every source file should produce at least one node.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cost\\r\\n\\r\\nNine agents. Roughly 1.1M subagent tokens for 170 documents. Budget for a session\\r\\nlimit on a corpus this size, and prefer resuming over restarting.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Relation to the levels model\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is a concrete, low-cost path to the Level 4 knowledge-graph rung that\\r\\n[[second-brain-levels]] deliberately parks. It does not change the standing call:\\r\\nai-os stays a Level 2 LLM-wiki brain, and graphify stays **on demand**. Available,\\r\\nnot adopted. See `references/second-brain-levels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source\'s own caveat cuts against the obvious next target: for code bases, stop\\r\\nat graphify and skip the Obsidian merge. That argues against pointing this at\\r\\n`projects/lsatu/`.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"a28f66","wall_time_seconds":0.2515287,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":97,"output":"\\r\\nName             PSIsContainer\\r\\n----             -------------\\r\\nclaude-code-docs          True\\r\\ndistillates               True\\r\\ngraphify-out              True\\r\\nvault                     True\\r\\nvault-b2                  True\\r\\nvault-b3                  True\\r\\nvault-run                 True\\r\\nvault-run-b2              True\\r\\nvault-run-b3              True\\r\\n_INVENTORY.md            False\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': orts\\\\vault-run-b2\\\\graphify-out\\\\.graphify_chunk_02.json\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run-b2\\\\graphify-out\\\\.graphify_chunk_01.json\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run-b2\\\\graphify-out\\\\.graphify_ast.json\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run-b2\\\\graphify-out\\\\.graphify_analysis.json\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run\\\\graphify-out\\\\graph.json\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run\\\\graphify-out\\\\graph.html\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run\\\\graphify-out\\\\.graphify_labels.json\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run\\\\graphify-out\\\\.graphify_extract.json\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run\\\\graphify-out\\\\.graphify_detect.json\\r\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run\\\\graphify-out\\\\.graphify_chunk_14.json\\r\\n"}'}]

Coverage:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Files | Student |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| 51 | `_other-sessions` (mixed historical) |\\r\\n| 17 | meghan |\\r\\n| 11 | david-salgado |\\r\\n| 10 | dakota |\\r\\n| 8 | lydia |\\r\\n| 7 | adriana-varea |\\r\\n| 6 | ethan |\\r\\n| 4 | sofia |\\r\\n| 2 each | yahunatan-zetvy, thaniuska-vivas, c-alexander, bella-lorenzo |\\r\\n| 1 each | nicole, lexi, favour |\\r\\n\\r\\nTranscripts only. No homework, practice-report, or email distillates exist (those\\r\\nsource folders are empty vault-wide).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Pipeline (the machine — keep and repurpose)\\r\\n\\r\\n- `vault-run/` (b1), `vault-run-b2/`, `vault-run-b3/` — each holds the build\\r\\n  scripts. b3 is the most complete: `merge.py`, `apply_stitch.py`,\\r\\n  `apply_corrections.py` + `corrections.json`, `wire_sources.py`,\\r\\n  `fix_vault_names.py`, `verify.py`, plus `graphify-out/`.\\r\\n- `graphify-out/` (top level) — `GRAPH_REPORT.md`, `graph.html`, `graph.json`,\\r\\n  `manifest.json`, `cost.json`, `cache/`.\\r\\n- `verify.py` is the trust-nothing checker: recomputes every count from disk.\\r\\n- Ship gate is `components: 1`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What this means for the build plan\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Phase 3 (unify the graph)** is a MERGE of four existing components plus a\\r\\n  fresh graphify pass, not a from-scratch build. The 1,152 shared real-file\\r\\n  targets are the natural seams to stitch on.\\r\\n- **Phase 2 (playbooks)** already has both inputs on disk: `corpus/Curriculum/`\\r\\n  (planned) and `distillates/` (delivered).\\r\\n- **`claude-code-docs`** is the one graph that is vendor material, not Joshua\'s.\\r\\n  Flag for a keep/scope decision at unify time (it may belong in the graph as\\r\\n  capability reference, or may be excluded as not-his-brain).\\r\\n- Nothing is dropped (Joshua\'s override). Unification + index + lenses is how\\r\\n  usability is achieved, not pruning.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"20b789","wall_time_seconds":0.2503106,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":62,"output":"graph-imports\\\\vault-run-b3\\\\graphify-out\\\\graph.json\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run-b2\\\\graphify-out\\\\graph.json\\ngraph-imports\\\\graphify-out\\\\graph.json\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault-run\\\\graphify-out\\\\graph.json\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault\\\\AGENTS.md\\ngraph-imports\\\\vault\\\\CLAUDE.md\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"9c8d94","wall_time_seconds":0.9958869,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json,collections,re\nroot=Path.cwd()\nfor p in Path('graph-imports').rglob('graph.json'):\n z=json.loads(p.read_text(encoding='utf-8'));nodes=z['nodes'];edges=z['links'];ids={n['id'] for n in nodes};adj={x:set() for x in ids};dangling=0\n for e in edges:\n  a,b=e.get('source'),e.get('target')\n  if a not in ids or b not in ids:dangling+=1;continue\n  adj[a].add(b);adj[b].add(a)\n remaining=set(ids);cs=[]\n while remaining:\n  stack=[remaining.pop()];size=0\n  while stack:\n   x=stack.pop();size+=1\n   for y in adj[x]&remaining:remaining.remove(y);stack.append(y)\n  cs.append(size)\n print(json.dumps({'graph':str(p),'components':len(cs),'largest_component':max(cs),'dangling_edges':dangling,'communities':len({str(n.get('community')) for n in nodes}),'relations':collections.Counter(e.get('relation',e.get('type','unknown')) for e in edges).most_common(5)}))\nfor d in ['vault','vault-b2','vault-b3','claude-code-docs']:\n fs=list((Path('graph-imports')/d).glob('*.md'));wiring=0;stub=0;sourcefield=0;known=0;missing=0\n for p in fs:\n  t=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8');stub+=len(t.split())<60;wiring+='## Source' in t\n  m=re.search(r'^source_file:\\s*[\"\\']?([^\\n\"\\']+)',t,re.M)\n  if m:\n   sourcefield+=1;v=m.group(1).strip()\n   if (root/v).exists():known+=1\n   else:missing+=1\n print(json.dumps({'folder':d,'top_md':len(fs),'under60words':stub,'source_sections':wiring,'source_file_fields':sourcefield,'literal_source_file_present':known,'literal_source_file_absent':missing}))\nprint(json.dumps({'project_claude_graphify_skill':(root/'.claude/skills/graphify/SKILL.md').exists(),'project_codex_graphify_skill':(root/'.agents/skills/graphify/SKILL.md').exists(),'root_graph_dir':(root/'graph').exists(),'root_graphify_out_dir':(root/'graphify-out').exists()}))\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:2000}));

Both vaults have Obsidian’s native Graph enabled. The old vault has saved display settings, but **no graph imports**. Its graph looks simpler because the vault is much smaller.

Current graph imports include four verified graph artifacts:

| Domain | Nodes | Edges |
|---|---:|---:|
| Claude documentation | 963 | 1,239 |
| Brain domains | 1,942 | 2,745 |
| Session distillates | 1,368 | 2,848 |
| Skills/agents | 750 | 1,174 |

Each artifact is individually connected with no dangling edges. This does not establish one unified, current whole-brain graph.

**Recommendation:** focused native graph views are the easiest useful improvement. Separate teaching/student/wiki views from generated imports and operational folders. Copying old force/zoom settings would mainly change appearance.

Source routing is the more substantial issue. Many graph metadata paths retain Mac prefixes or lack the `students/` prefix; most resolve after straightforward remapping, but some remain unverified. These checks do not prove the actual clickable source links are broken. Historical inventory counts also differ from current exports.

No edits made. Graphify availability through the session’s skills is separate from whether its CLI/project activation is presently verified.

One authoritative home; the router points here. Updated by hand when facts change.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Name:** Joshua. Solo founder/operator — no team, no VA.\\r\\n- **What he does:** LSAT instructor. Runs **Aspiring Attorneys**, a 1-on-1 LSAT tutoring/coaching business, and is building **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform. North star: take everything *around* the tutoring sessions off his plate so he only shows up and teaches.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"540f6a","wall_time_seconds":0.5388248,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2100,"output":"\\r\\n**Eliminate first.** *\\"What happens if we just stop doing this?\\"* You\'d be surprised how many processes exist because they always have. Reports nobody reads. Approval steps that add no value. **If nobody would notice it disappeared, kill it. Don\'t automate waste.**\\r\\n\\r\\n**Automate second.** Apply the **60/30/10 Golden Rule:**\\r\\n- ~60% fully automated (no human touch)\\r\\n- ~30% AI-assisted (AI does the work, human reviews before it goes out)\\r\\n- ~10% stays manual (too nuanced, too risky, or too rare)\\r\\n\\r\\nThis ratio normalizes expectations. **Full automation is rarely the goal.** If someone promises 100% on anything meaningful, they\'re selling you something.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Delegate third.** If a process can\'t hit 60/30/10 — too complex, too variable, too judgment-dependent — delegate to a person. Not everything should be automated.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe key: nothing stays as-is. Every process gets killed, automated, or handed off.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Map the Process\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore you touch any tool, write every step on paper. Five elements per process:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Trigger** — what kicks it off (form submission, calendar event, email, time of day)\\r\\n- **Data Sources** — where information comes from (CRM, spreadsheet, inbox)\\r\\n- **Data Transformations** — how data changes shape (reformatting, filtering, combining)\\r\\n- **Decision Points** — where it branches (if qualified, do X; if not, do Y)\\r\\n- **Destination** — where output goes (back to CRM, email, Slack, document)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Rule:** *if you can\'t explain it to a person, you can\'t explain it to an AI.* The map forces clarity. Skip this step and you\'ll build something that sort of works but breaks in weird ways.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. The Autonomy Spectrum\\r\\n\\r\\nEach step gets an autonomy level:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Level | Name | What Happens |\\r\\n|-------|------|-------------|\\r\\n| L0 | Manual | No AI. Human does it. |\\r\\n| L1 | Suggested | AI suggests, human decides every step. |\\r\\n| L2 | Drafted | AI drafts, human reviews and edits. |\\r\\n| L3 | Supervised | Rules set, AI runs, human validates. |\\r\\n| L4 | Autonomous | AI handles end-to-end. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Governing principle: default to the LOWEST level that works.**\\r\\n\\r\\nMost people get this backwards. They hear \\"AI automation\\" and jump to L4. That\'s where things go wrong. Boring is Beautiful. Deterministic beats non-deterministic. **Workflows beat agents.** If a decision doesn\'t HAVE to be made by AI, don\'t let AI make it.\\r\\n\\r\\nPush autonomy up only when you\'ve proven the lower level works.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Tie It to a KPI\\r\\n\\r\\nIf your automation doesn\'t move a number, why are you building it?\\r\\n\\r\\n**The Three Buckets** (every business metric falls into one):\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Get more customers** — content, prospecting, outreach, ads, lead gen\\r\\n2. **Make each customer worth more** — premium services at lower cost, upselling, retention\\r\\n3. **Cut costs** — eliminate drudgery, reduce errors, boost productivity\\r\\n\\r\\n**Specific KPIs** are tied to the individual automation: response time, error rate, tickets per month, conversion rate, time-to-completion.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf your automation doesn\'t improve a metric in one of the three buckets, stop. *\\"Because it\'s cool\\"* isn\'t a business case.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Layer 3 — MACHINE (How to Build and Operate)\\r\\n\\r\\nYou\'ve got the thinking (Mindset) and the decisions (Method). Now you build and run the thing. Two halves: BUILD and OPERATE.\\r\\n\\r\\n### BUILD\\r\\n\\r\\n#### 1. The Lego Principle\\r\\n\\r\\nSmallest possible steps. One input, one output per block. Output of block 1 becomes input of block 2.\\r\\n\\r\\nStart with **zero-AI steps first**. Get the deterministic pieces working — data fetching, formatting, routing. Then layer in AI where actually needed.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis makes the project less overwhelming and lets you validate as you go. If block 3 produces garbage, you know exactly where to look. **Modularity is freedom.**\\r\\n\\r\\n#### 2. The Assembly Line\\r\\n\\r\\nEach AI step does one specialized job. Like workers on an assembly line.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Don\'t build a generalist.** One model call for copywriting. Another for reasoning. Another for classification. Keep them separate. Easier to debug, swap models, adjust prompts.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### 3. The Validation Chain\\r\\n\\r\\nValidate each step\'s output before chaining. **Do NOT build the whole pipeline and test end-to-end.** That\'s a recipe for \\"it doesn\'t work and I have no idea why.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nBuild step 1. Run it. Confirm output. Build step 2. Run with step 1\'s actual output. Confirm. Chain. Add step 3. This is how POCs actually work.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### 4. The Iteration Mindset\\r\\n\\r\\nThere\'s no finished product — especially with AI. Deterministic scripts CAN be done (a CSV reformatter, sure). AI steps are always evolving. New models. New capabilities. The prompt that was optimal six months ago is verbose and expensive today.\\r\\n\\r\\nShip the POC. Get real-usage feedback. Expand. Iterate. **Perfectionism is the enemy of deployment.**\\r\\n\\r\\n### OPERATE\\r\\n\\r\\n#### 5. The Bike Method\\r\\n\\r\\nRoll out in phases, like teaching a kid to ride.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Phase 1 — Training wheels.** Run manually. Watch everything. Correct mistakes by hand.\\r\\n- **Phase 2 — Guided.** Automation runs but you review every output. It drafts, doesn\'t send.\\r\\n- **Phase 3 — Watched.** Runs autonomously. You monitor. Alerts for anomalies. Periodic batch review.\\r\\n- **Phase 4 — Hands-off.** Helmet on, go ride.\\r\\n\\r\\nEven at 90% confidence, roll out 10% of volume first. Watch a week. Add 20% more. Like drug trials — not full dose to everyone day one.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse confidence thresholds: high → auto-send, medium → draft queue, low → escalate to human. Tighten or loosen as data accumulates.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### 6. The Intern Rule\\r\\n\\r\\nTreat AI like a brand-new hire on day one.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Own identity.** Its own email, accounts, credentials. Never yours.\\r\\n- **Read-only by default.** View-only until you\'ve proven write access is needed.\\r\\n- **Never impersonates you.** Signs off as \\"[your name]\'s AI assistant.\\"\\r\\n- **No personal credentials.** No passwords, bank info, personal logins.\\r\\n- **Full audit trail.** Visibility into everything it did, spent, created, deleted.\\r\\n- **Scoped permissions.** API keys with minimal scope. Exactly what\'s needed, nothing more.\\r\\n\\r\\n*\\"You wouldn\'t trust someone you just met with your bank account.\\"*\\r\\n\\r\\n#### 7. The Kill Switch\\r\\n\\r\\nMonitor what\'s running. If an automation consistently needs patches, produces low-quality output, or costs more to maintain than it saves — **tear it down.** Dismantle. Delete.\\r\\n\\r\\nDon\'t fall into the sunk cost trap. *\\"But I spent three weeks building this\\"* is not a reason to keep something running that doesn\'t work. **Good operators know when to build AND when to destroy.** The kill switch is just as important as the launch button.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Governing Principles\\r\\n\\r\\nThree principles that sit above everything else. When in doubt, return to these.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Boring is beautiful.** Predictable beats clever. Default to the simplest, most deterministic approach that gets the job done.\\r\\n2. **Deterministic steps can be finished. AI steps are always evolving.** Set expectations — yours and your client\'s — accordingly. A rule-based filter is done. An AI classifier needs tuning forever.\\r\\n3. **Fail fast, learn faster.** Get to your first 10 mistakes safely and quickly. Real learning lives there, not in planning, not in your first 10 successes.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Branch Frameworks (future hooks)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe 3Ms is the mothership. Specific topics go deeper in dedicated frameworks. Most aren\'t built into this kit yet. They\'ll grow into `references/` over time:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The Data Retrieval Hierarchy** — Filters, SQL, Full Context, RAG: when to use which\\r\\n- **The Integration Ladder** — API, CLI, Browser Automation, Scraping: hierarchy of reliability\\r\\n- **The Error Handling Playbook** — What to do when things break (and they will)\\r\\n- **The Model Selection Guide** — How to pick the right model for the right job\\r\\n- **The Context Engineering Framework** — How to feed AI the right information at the right time\\r\\n- **The Discovery Playbook** — How to run discovery with a client or team before building\\r\\n- **The Security and Permissions Playbook** — Access control, audit trails, risk management\\r\\n\\r\\nEach plugs into the 3Ms at specific points. Start here, branch out as you need depth.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n> *Adapted from The Three Ms of AI™. © 2026 Nate Herk. All rights reserved.*\\r\\n> *Full breakdown with diagrams and examples: [insert link to YouTube companion video / public-facing page when published].*\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"ab998e","wall_time_seconds":0.5065896,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1893,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: reference\\r\\nsubject: Automation & Workflow Analysis Joshua\'s tutoring operation\\r\\nupdated: 2026-07-20\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Automation & Workflow Analysis — Joshua\'s tutoring operation\\r\\n\\r\\n**Built:** 2026-07-18. **Source:** 944 emails Joshua authored (mid-2025 → mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout) + the current skill library + business structure. **Status:** analysis + build backlog.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Build status (updated 2026-07-20)\\r\\n\\r\\n| Item | Status |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| **A. Morning brief** | **Built** — `morning-brief` skill. Drafts only, payment flag-only, logs to `wiki/send-ledger.md`. **Not yet scheduled** — runs on request until Joshua opts into a cron. |\\r\\n| **B. Reschedule assistant** | Not built. Now the highest-leverage remaining item (25% of his mail). |\\r\\n| **C. Payment follow-up** | Not built. `morning-brief` deliberately flags payment rather than drafting it, so this stays a conscious gap. |\\r\\n| **D. Session-notes ingest** | **Built** — `ingest` skill + `system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py`. Maps 44 of 52 transcripts; the rest are named gaps, not failures. |\\r\\n| **E. Student roster** | **Built** — `system/scripts/build-roster.py` → `students/roster.generated.md`. Implemented as a generated markdown table rather than an Obsidian Base, since the data is small and a script is portable to Codex. |\\r\\n| **F. Gone-quiet detector** | Not built, but now cheap: the roster carries last-session dates, which is the data it needed. |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe apology-tax finding below (19%) is what ranked A first; the session-mining\\r\\nsweep in `brainstorms/2026-07-19-session-mining-reflection.md` independently\\r\\nre-derived the same ranking.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is a map of how Joshua actually spends his email/ops time and where a skill, workflow, or automation removes or assists that work. It separates what\'s a real GAP (build it) from what\'s already COVERED (a skill exists — the job is to use or sharpen it). Read this before building anything so you don\'t duplicate the existing library.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. What the corpus shows about his workload\\r\\n\\r\\n944 authored messages, classified by workflow (messages can hit more than one category):\\r\\n\\r\\n| Workflow | Msgs | Share | Mechanical? |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Scheduling / calendar | 239 | 25% | High — reschedules, invites, availability |\\r\\n| Homework delivery | 185 | 20% | Medium — per-student judgment + a link |\\r\\n| Encouragement / check-in | 167 | 18% | Low — genuine, but templatable openers |\\r\\n| **Apology / delay** | **181** | **19%** | High — he is chronically behind on replies |\\r\\n| Consultation (reminder + follow-up) | 152 | 16% | High — near-identical templates |\\r\\n| Inbound lead reply | 91 | 10% | High — same \\"yes I\'m available, here\'s the link\\" |\\r\\n| Payment / billing | 71 | 8% | High — invoices, payment nudges |\\r\\n| Session notes / recap | 47 | 5% | Medium — depends on his notes |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Relationship shape:** 155 distinct recipients. 43 are active relationships (≥5 messages) — the real student book. 71 are one-offs (leads/prospects who emailed once). So ~half his inbound is lead traffic that never converts, and he still hand-replies to it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**The headline finding:** **19% of everything he writes is an apology for being slow to reply.** That is not a voice problem, it\'s a throughput problem. The single highest-leverage automation is anything that keeps him from falling behind on his inbox in the first place. Speed of reply is the bottleneck, not quality.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Already COVERED (don\'t rebuild — use or sharpen)\\r\\n\\r\\nHe has a strong library already. These map directly onto the workflows above:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`consultation-inquiry-drafts`** — daily sweep, drafts consultation-link replies to new inquiries. Covers most of \\"inbound lead reply.\\"\\r\\n- **`consultation-followup-drafts`** — daily, drafts follow-ups from consultation Gemini notes. Covers \\"consultation follow-up.\\"\\r\\n- **`inbound-tutoring-replies`** — drafts replies to leads across channels (FB, Superprof, TutorOcean, Reddit, email).\\r\\n- **`guided-drill-assignment`** + **`lsat-u-platform-actions`** — transcript → pick a drill → assign on-platform → verify notification. Covers the platform side of \\"homework delivery.\\"\\r\\n- **`gws-*` suite** (gmail-triage, gmail-reply, gmail-send, calendar-insert, calendar-agenda, drive, docs, workflow-meeting-prep, workflow-weekly-digest, etc.) — the primitives for almost any email/calendar/drive automation already exist. Most gaps below are *orchestration* over these, not new capability.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Implication:** the gap is less \\"build new skills\\" and more \\"wire the existing ones into a daily rhythm that runs without him asking.\\" A lot of these are described as \\"daily\\" but require him to invoke them. The leverage is making them proactive.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. The GAPS — ranked by leverage (build these)\\r\\n\\r\\nLeverage = (time saved or dropped-ball prevented) × (frequency) × (how mechanical). Highest first.\\r\\n\\r\\n### TIER 1 — build first\\r\\n\\r\\n**A. Daily inbox triage + draft queue (\\"morning brief\\").**\\r\\nThe fix for the 19% apology problem. One scheduled run each morning that: sweeps Gmail for anything needing a reply, classifies it (lead / active student / logistics / payment / consultation), and produces a single ranked queue of *drafts* — using the existing consultation/inbound skills where they apply, and the newly-enriched `wiki/teaching-voice.md` for tone. He reviews and sends. Never auto-sends (his rule). This collapses \\"I\'m behind on email\\" into a 15-minute daily review.\\r\\n- *Builds on:* `gws-gmail-triage`, `gws-gmail-read`, the three drafting skills, `teaching-voice.md`.\\r\\n- *New work:* the orchestration + classification + a single digest surface. Consider a scheduled cloud agent (`/schedule`) so it runs before he wakes up.\\r\\n\\r\\n**B. Reschedule / scheduling assistant.**\\r\\n25% of his mail. When a student asks to move a session, the move is: check the calendar, propose/confirm a slot, send the invite, update anything downstream. Highly mechanical, currently all hand-done.\\r\\n- *Builds on:* `gws-calendar-agenda`, `gws-calendar-insert`, Cal.com.\\r\\n- *New work:* a skill that reads a reschedule request, checks availability, and drafts the confirmation + invite in his voice (\\"No worries at all. It happens...\\").\\r\\n\\r\\n**C. Payment / billing follow-up.**\\r\\n71 messages, and money that slips is money lost. A skill that tracks who owes what (from Stripe + invoice emails) and drafts the gentle nudge.\\r\\n- *Builds on:* Stripe connection (in `connections.md`), `gws-gmail`.\\r\\n- *New work:* reconcile sessions-delivered vs. paid, surface gaps, draft the reminder. Sensitive — draft only, never auto-send.\\r\\n\\r\\n### TIER 2 — build next\\r\\n\\r\\n**D. Session-notes → student-folder ingest (the deferred transcript pipeline).**\\r\\nThe 184 Gemini Doc transcripts + ongoing ones. Parse the `gemini-notes@google.com` subjects (student + date), fetch each Doc via `gws drive`, distill to a note, file into `students/<name>/`. This is the pipeline scoped in the prior handoff but not built. Turns his recorded sessions into durable per-student memory.\\r\\n- *Builds on:* `gws-drive`, `gws-docs`, `second-brain-ingest`, the student-folder structure.\\r\\n- *Note:* notes ≠ transcript ≠ homework (per the prior handoff\'s reframe). File them distinctly.\\r\\n\\r\\n**E. Student roster + status dashboard.**\\r\\nHe has 43 active students scattered across email threads.
